# Scream Simulator development roadmap

## Keep the Celestial Organ on the avatar's back

- In a disposable Studio run, the Academy Megaphone's 13 parts stayed welded to the R15 right hand during a synthetic cast. The normal Gear controls removed and re-equipped it, and it returned after respawn. In the same baseline build the catalog's back-mounted Celestial Organ appeared as a handheld speaker with 14 right-hand welds when its authored template was unavailable.
- The standard build now supplies a 13-part gold-and-violet organ pack, based on the approved concept. A back item waits for the torso on both rig types. The portable model uses massless, noncolliding parts and leaves the hand free. Price, power and authored-template precedence follow the existing catalog and equipment rules.
- The focused Luau mock failed against the old code, which mounted a back item before the torso was ready. It passes for both torso names, welds, removal and repeat sync. Two negative controls catch a restored handheld fallback and an early fallback to the hand. Studio evidence is kept under `build/megaphone_visual_native`.
- In the disposable candidate, all 13 Organ parts were welded to the R15 upper torso, the right hand was empty, and the pipes were visible from behind. The actual Megaphone buy-and-equip action still fitted all 13 parts to the right hand. Independent review, 198 local suites, 99 Luau compilations, and the City and Hall builds passed. Both Studio sessions closed. R6 coverage used a mock rig. The Organ check staged an equipped item and did not play through level 90.

## Keep the first Gear action fully reachable on narrow screens

- Studio reproduced a clipped **GUIDE TO MAR** action at 455 × 218 usable touch-emulated pixels. The content window was 40 pixels high and clipped the bottom 10 pixels of the 44-pixel button. A pointer tested below the clip hit the menu instead of the action.
- The narrow landscape menu now uses the sidebar. It fits two tab columns when there is enough height for three rows and three columns at shorter supported heights. Cards measure the scroller's current width, so resizing across the column change rebuilds wrapping and action positions. In a separate candidate Studio run at 455 × 218, the 154-pixel pane showed the full 44-pixel Gear button, the product text and six fitted tabs. A click four pixels above the button bottom activated the guide. Focused mocks also cover 448 × 150 and rebuilding on a width change.
- One fresh temporary profile completed three Garden holds and six Beach pickups. The first Gear card showed **NEED 20 COINS** after Garden, opened Beach, and exposed **ACCEPT** then **GUIDE**. The route crossed the marked Garden PvP area and reached the beach without repositioning or a manual ramp recovery. Normal claims earned 130 coins. **GEAR READY** opened Gear, and **BUY & EQUIP** spent 100, equipped the Academy Megaphone and reported a 1.06 multiplier. This baseline-layout run could click the center of the partly clipped button. The game state and UI verified the purchase and bonus. Native evidence and screenshots are under `build/current_earned_gear_native`. Independent review, 13 menu negative controls, 198 local suites, 99 Luau compilations and the City and Hall builds passed.

## Keep the first Rookie win visible beside a notice

- A fresh temporary level-1 profile used the Guide and walked its live route from Academy spawn into Rookie Training in 14.80 seconds. With staged power through the production beam controls, two casts earned the first knockout, 48 XP and five coins. The tracker recorded **1/3 fighters**. At 455 × 218 usable touch-emulated pixels, the adjacent **NOTICE** shortcut reduced the guide to 52 × 46 pixels and left it showing **CLEAR / Aim**, with the earned count missing.
- The compact training guide now keeps the fighter count as a third line beside the sparring instruction and Clear action. The guide and notice each fit their own touch target at that viewport. Focused checks cover the normal route, active and resting fighter cues, notice lane and quest timer. The negative control restores the missing count.
- A second fresh run replayed the walk, first hit and knockout in the exact candidate fixture. The 52 × 46 guide displayed fitted **CLEAR / En route / 1/3** beside the notice shortcut at the reward. The middle line reports the current fighter or route state. Clicking **NOTICE** exposed the fitted Rookie reward after the queued arming message. The prior emulator preset was restored and both disposable sessions closed. The paired screenshots and native records are under `build/rookie_first_run_native`. Independent review and 29 focused negative controls passed. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds.
- This is the first spar and reward handoff. The end-to-end path into the first earned gear, unassisted discovery and real voice still need their own playtests.

## Give the Academy's Hall portal a recognizable entrance

- The City portal previously appeared as an unlabeled translucent rectangle. The shared travel-fixture entry now has an open cyan light frame, a gold waveform and a **RESONANCE HALL / PVP ARENA** sign. The floor accents sit above the visible Academy paving while the interaction point and arrivals stay fixed.
- The first Studio candidate clipped Academy and Rookie wayfinding. The final sign is narrower and lower. Both destinations were readable from the settled 567 × 241 spawn with the ordinary HUD. When a resident triggered Talk, that button could still cover the first letters of the Hall sign. The close approach showed the full Hall sign, with minor overlap at the edge of the Rookie sign from that angle.
- Native checks confirmed the 15 render-only parts did not change rays, floor, walking or the portal hold. The avatar walked through on sandstone and F showed the fitting rehearsal notice without moving the avatar. See the paired captures and measurements under `build/academy_hall_gateway_native`. The gateway appears in the travel fixtures. Independent review and ten negative controls passed, including controls for altered collision settings, prompt distance and buried flooring. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and both travel build modes. All disposable Studio windows closed. Actual cross-place transfer and physical phone readability remain open.

## Verify Hall damage stops at the safe apron

- Two Studio clients used the linked Hall rehearsal with temporary profiles. One accepted beam through the normal Action remote dealt 12 HP on the PvP floor, zero for 1.10 seconds on the apron, then 17 HP after the defender returned to the floor. The beam stayed active, clear and in range at the sampled points. Spawn protection had expired. The same-run apron control isolates the zone rule while keeping the attack and participants constant.
- Only the defender received the two private hit messages. The fitted **BEAM HIT YOU** cue could finish its existing display on the apron, with no new hit or health loss there. Finishing the cast set the cooldown deadline. A virtual F hold at the apron portal showed the local message and left the avatar in place. See [native Hall results](../tests/evidence/hall-playable-pvp-boundary-native-2026-11-22.json).
- Independent review confirmed the evidence. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds. The focused Hall map, portal, travel and beam-contact checks also passed. No gameplay changes were required. The next external gates are actual voice on two eligible accounts, including a physical phone, and transfer between two approved places. This fixture did not exercise Hall knockout or internet multiplayer. All disposable Studio sessions closed.

## Rehearse City training and Hall combat around the portals

- The travel-only pair exercises landings and prompts without the game runtime. The new playable pair reuses those same portal landings alongside the City quest/training systems and Hall combat HUD. The files require Studio with zero place and universe IDs, temporary profiles and an unconfigured commerce catalog. The existing travel-only and local Hall preview modes remain available.
- The former Hall fall adapter could target the practice deck when the cross-place geometry omits it. The playable Hall now recovers to its safe apron, and its map and Rules panel show the City portal without a practice-deck destination. Focused checks and independent review cover both Hall map modes, missing-deck fall recovery and the travel gate. Full verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and both playable builds.
- Native City showed the Rookie objective, four fighters and ten quests. Native Hall showed its Rules panel and switched from safe to PvP after walking beyond the blue line. Both F prompts gave local feedback without moving the avatar. Falls staged at z240 and z800 returned the Hall avatar to the apron with renewed protection. See `build/playable_travel_native/native-results.json`. The final build regenerated provenance after the geometry-only City guard moved before map construction. The playable runtime was unchanged by that edit. Both disposable sessions closed.
- Actual teleport and shared progress still need two approved test places and separate persistence authorization. Run `python tests/build_sustained_review.py --travel-local-qa` for the playable local pair.

## Verify compact combat Close beside a scrollable notice

- Two local Studio clients at 567 × 241 usable pixels used staged input through the normal attack remote. A private server hit reduced the defender from 480 to 457 HP while Gear and a scrollable notice were open. A test pointer reached the notice, then reached the adjacent 44 × 44 **HIT / CLOSE** button during damage. The menu closed and the fitted **BEAM HIT YOU** label returned about 101 ms after the hit. The notice remained visible after closing.
- The multiplayer clients kept the desktop layout. A separate touch-emulated solo at 455 × 218 staged the private cue after spawn protection. The notice ended six pixels before the fitted Close target. A virtual mouse click closed the menu and restored the fitted combat cue. The first staged cue during protection was suppressed. Physical tap, post-close **NOTICE** shortcut access on touch, and real damage in touch emulation remain unverified.
- Independent review confirmed the result, requiring no gameplay change. Focused notice and combat integration passed 1,187 checks and 93 negative controls. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds. The previous simulator preset was restored and disposable Studio sessions closed. See [native results](../tests/evidence/compact-defender-menu-pointer-native-2026-11-22.json). Next, test real speech and ordinary proximity chat on two eligible accounts, including a phone, in an approved isolated build.

## Verify repeated overlapping Stadium hits and cleanup

- A disposable build from `de9228a` staged two level-100 attackers and a defender in the Stadium. Two Studio attackers sent synthetic input through the normal Action RemoteEvent. A third Studio client was the defender. The longer first run reached a knockout during the second window and no damage to the defender in the third. Shorter casts preserved the same target for all three windows without forcing health or repositioning between windows.
- In the short run both attackers dealt 51, 51 and 49 HP each across the three windows. Their simultaneous casts lasted about 0.57 seconds per window. The defender showed fitted **BEAM HIT YOU** every time. Every client received both end events, with zero active attacks and zero parented beam parts or template roots at the cleanup checkpoints. Server heartbeat p95 was about 18 ms during idle, the combat window, and recovery. It is a local interval sample, with different client frame cadences on the same host. See [the fixture results](../tests/evidence/stadium-repeated-casts-native-2026-11-22.json).
- Independent evidence review cleared. The focused combat check passed 412 integration assertions and 50 negative controls. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds. All disposable Studio processes closed. The owner playtest now points to current City landmarks, third-person controls and solid scenery. Next verify pointer activation of **HIT / CLOSE** alongside a scrollable notice at the narrow supported touch layout. Real speech, device performance, crowded internet matches, and separate approved travel and save tests remain open.

## Reduce repeated reads during resident animation

- A two-resident update using the actual frame callback with mocked engine inputs read the four sampled pose attributes 32 times and sampled server time twice. The updated callback reads the same attributes eight times and samples server time once, only when a resident is due. Work, gesture, reading and applause still use current attributes each update.
- Focused lifecycle and pose checks passed with two negative controls. Studio found 18 resident tags in both disposable City builds. The sampled Nick joint changed 125/42/10 times at near/mid/far distances over 126 frames per distance on the baseline and 125/41/10 on the candidate. Nick's practice animation and local ball appeared in both. The two disposable sessions closed. See `build/resident_motion_native/native-results.json` and the paired Nick screenshots. These counts establish distance cadence, with CPU time and multiplayer load still unmeasured. Independent review and full local verification passed, including 198 suites, 99 production compilations, and City and Hall builds.
- Next, use the existing device and multiplayer playtest gates to inspect first-beam comprehension, crowded combat and data handoff in the exact candidate. Approved test places are still needed for actual transfer and separately authorized persistence.

## Apply the City surface palette to solid terrain

- The original finishing pass colored solid surfaces but skipped their authored material grades. Native baseline and a failing focused assertion confirmed SmoothPlastic on the Academy plateau, island, stadium tier, garden terrace and main pier. The finishing pass now applies the planned sandstone, concrete, grass, timber, rock and basalt grades. It preserves each changed collider's previous contact properties and any existing custom override.
- Same-camera passes in the disposable City compare the Academy and Timber Pier. Studio compared all 174 graded parts across the before and after builds. Of those, 167 changed material. Contact properties, transforms, sizes, collision flags and ray hits matched. The avatar walked 24 studs along the pier at a stable height and reported WoodPlanks underfoot. See `build/graded_surfaces_native/native-results.json` and the paired screenshots. Both disposable sessions closed. Full local verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations, and the City and Hall builds. The focused suite and independent review passed, including 19 negative controls. Three exercise the material guard and both ways physical properties could be lost.
- Next, profile a representative resident-animation workload and remove confirmed redundant engine reads if the behavior stays equivalent. Physical touch, real voice and a representative multiplayer soak remain open, along with approved places for actual travel and isolated persistence.

## Stage and verify safe City and Hall arrivals

- The travel-only pair previously used ordinary spawns for inbound travelers. Hall arrivals now start inside the safe apron facing the arena boundary, with the City portal behind them. City arrivals land at the Academy facing the training courts. Only the configured peer's engine-reported source IDs select a landing in the test-ID builds. The zero-ID rehearsal stages the same landings for local inspection.
- Native reset before portal use reproduced stale arrival guidance. The fix clears that character's arrival banner on removal and preserves newer portal feedback. Native Studio verified the Hall apron, Academy spawn, banner fit and ordinary respawns after reset. See `build/world-travel/native-first-arrival-initial.json`, `build/world-travel/native-first-arrival-final.json` (City before a trailing-newline edit) and `build/world-travel/native-first-arrival-exact-hall.json` (exact Hall). Both real F holds updated the notice without moving the avatar. Disposable Studio sessions were closed.
- Independent review and 26 simulated travel assertions passed, with seven negative controls. Full local verification passed 198 suites and 99 production compilations, including City, Hall and both travel-only builds, on unchanged test inputs. The fixture covers direct joins, incorrect source IDs, duplicate arrival and respawn, a replaced first character, stale banner cleanup and simulated transport failure. Actual transfer still requires two distinct place IDs in the same approved nonproduction experience. These travel-only builds carry no gameplay profiles, combat or commerce.

## Build the City and Hall travel rehearsal pair

- The existing City and Hall candidates had no cross-place test path. The isolated, travel-only builder now generates both world fixtures using the current City base geometry and the Hall arch. The Academy prompt points to Resonance Hall, and the Hall arch points to City training. The standard City project and the previous local Hall preview retain their existing behavior.
- A zero-ID rehearsal uses native prompts and local on-screen feedback. Only the explicit test-ID build can request `TeleportAsync`, after verifying Studio and the configured source and universe. There are no progression, voice or commerce scripts in either travel-only artifact. Retrying after failure requires a new prompt activation. Per-player pending state rejects duplicate prompts and stale failure events.
- Focused Luau checks passed 14 assertions and three controls with simulated transport. Full builds now include the rehearsal pair. Actual cross-place transport still needs an approved two-place nonproduction experience. Durable progression, the historical writer fleet, real voice and physical device validation remain separate gates.
- Both exact rehearsal artifacts ran in native Studio. City spawned on AcademyPlateau0 eight studs from the new prompt. Hall reached the arch after a scripted walk of about 68 studs. At each end the real F hold showed fitted rehearsal feedback and left the avatar in place. The Hall travel rehearsal contains no practice deck. Evidence: `build/world-travel/city-native-final.json` and `build/world-travel/hall-native-final.json`. All disposable Studio sessions closed.
- Independent review passed. Full aggregate verification passed 198 suites, 99 production compilations and City, Hall and travel rehearsal builds on unchanged inputs. The distinct-ID builder guard rejected an equal-place argument, and the new travel sources compiled separately.

## Preserve City quest progress through a Hall profile

- The prior isolated writer probe on `2a6e975` paired the Hall preview's empty catalog with normal saving. It reproduced quest state erasure while retaining coins, XP and level. The Hall configuration now keeps a reference to the canonical City catalog for profile sanitization while leaving local quest lookup and offers empty. Bootstrap omits saved City quests and discoveries from Hall state packets. The real Hall review continues to use temporary profiles with storage disabled.
- The isolated acquisition/release/City-reload case now preserves four seeded states: partial, completed-unclaimed, claimed and discovery-only. Deleting the Hall catalog alias restores the original simulated loss. The actual Bootstrap state fixture keeps City data visible in City, suppresses both fields in Hall and leaves the stored state intact. Removing either packet filter fails independently.
- Focused checks passed 46 historical writer assertions, the current and Hall writer probes, 12 negative controls, 245 state/notice integration assertions and 20 controls, and 23 simulated profile handoff plus eight arrival-classifier checks. Live ownership and compatibility across actual destination builds remain separate release gates.
- In the exact native Hall candidate the active catalog was empty and all ten IDs remained in the saved catalog. Real Garden and Beach accept/claim actions produced no quest or reward on a fresh temporary profile. The **RULES** tab showed matching Hall directions without City quest cards. Both actual local portal prompts worked after scripted traversal. Candidate SHA-256: `cd7515bbaef7212c6ab20ce16f12c36968612f18c6810413ac34e60492365ba0`. Native evidence: `build/hall_quest_isolation_native/native-results.json`. The disposable Studio session closed. Full aggregate passed 197 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds on unchanged inputs.
- The next actual cross-place test still needs both destination IDs in an approved nonproduction experience and two travel-test builds. The historical writer audit below records the earlier failure and remains a deployment gate. Product saves, device microphone and physical touch were outside the native check.

## Verify the City profile contract before connecting the Hall

- The guarded Hall uses same-place prompt travel. Its generated catalog is empty because the review overrides storage to nil. The existing writer fixture paired that exact catalog override with normal profile acquisition on an isolated simulated store. It erased four representative City quest states during acquisition and left the 177 coins, level 20 and seven XP unchanged. Release and normal City reload did not restore the quest states. Canonical City writers still preserve the catalog in the existing acquisition/release/reload probe.
- The writer suite's timeout control still targeted the older one-argument load signature, so it stopped before exercising timeout detection. It now hangs the actual two-argument entry in the finite subprocess control. The 46 historical assertions, current writer probe, 11 negative controls, 23 simulated handoff checks and eight arrival-classifier checks passed. Local developer access preflight passed for the reviewed disabled configuration. Full aggregate passed 197 suites, 99 production compilations and City and Hall builds on unchanged inputs. Local normal City artifact SHA-256: `3242e611b939e83d0f7b68f396b9ff36f0ef8e56badccee2c30c85d4e91aeee2`. Its writer-fleet gate remains `BLOCKED_UNVERIFIED_WRITER_FLEET`.
- The release handoff distinguishes the Studio-supported same-experience travel-only test from a separately authorized real persistence test. The minimum external setup is approval for one separate nonproduction experience with City and Hall destinations. Deployment-shaped builds and matched canonical catalog are still needed. Studio-only Hall artifacts and an unverified writer fleet remain outside the release path.

## Open Gear directly from the earned upgrade cue

- At 567 × 241 usable pixels on `77c470a`, a disposable Studio profile started with 80 coins and five Beach bottles. The real prompt completed the quest and Claim raised the balance to 130. The fitted **GEAR READY** Menu button still reopened Quests and showed zero Megaphone controls. After visiting Settings it reopened Settings with the same cue. Baseline: `build/compact_first_feedback/native-baseline.json`.
- While the current affordability cue is visible, the existing Menu button and B shortcut open Gear at the top. The usual tab picker remains available, and Menu still closes the panel when open. Once the cue clears, Menu resumes the last selected tab. Quest rewards and actual gear entitlement continue through the server.
- Independent review cleared the change. Notice/Client tests passed 751 mocked assertions with 41 negative controls, including wrong-tab routing and stale scroll. Focused action feedback passed 27 checks and 11 controls.
- The exact candidate replayed the same Beach claim. Clicking **GEAR READY** or pressing B opened **GEAR & BEAM COLOR** at scroll zero. ## Keep beam geometry and particle travel in sync

- In the native `33cd1b9` renderer fixture, 12 overlapping level-50 and level-100 beams sized and posed their imported instances on every step. From actual asset contents, each mixed update attempted 162 imported property writes. With packet input held for six render steps, 135 of those per step on average were eligible to be skipped. Procedural paths, texture animation and mode updates run separately.
- The same native baseline reproduced stale Hollow Purple particle speed at a fixed width of five studs. Shortening a cast from 30 to 12 studs left emitter speed at 15.472 studs/sec instead of the scaled 6.189.
- The renderer now refreshes imported transforms on a different origin, direction, length, width or distance band. Particle speed follows the actual travel length independently of beam-width changes. Near Firebeam geometry refreshes on re-entry from far detail if it moved while distant.
- Independent review cleared the exact diff. The mocked milestone suite passed 7,158 checks and 24 negative controls. The controls reproduced stale travel speed, unnecessary steady writes and missing near-detail refresh. Pool composition passed 115,412 assertions in independent review.
- The exact candidate updated all four emitters across six fixed-width steps, including an unchanged packet and the two length clamps. For the reproduced 30-to-12-stud move, it changed speed from 15.472 to 6.189 studs/sec. Firebeam matched six baseline geometry snapshots, including all 16 attachment offsets, far-to-near refresh, and cross-cast reuse. Both disposable Studio sessions closed. Full verification passed 29 pure or synthetic suites, 168 mocked suites, 99 production compilations and Rojo builds on unchanged verification inputs. Evidence: `build/beam_geometry_native/native-candidate.json`. Candidate SHA-256: `140e13075dcf9a837503a283ce494fa827fb26421f6b8ffa8c04c2a32b3bf339`.
- Isolated Studio renderer timings varied. In the 12-beam fixed-width held-packet case, median script time was 0.253 ms before and 0.224 ms after, while p95 rose from 0.637 to 0.673 ms. The controlled result supports fewer idle writes. Frame-rate, production texture permissions, real voice, internet multiplayer and physical-phone performance remain unverified.

## Drain active purchase writes before server shutdown returns

- The audit reproduced a shutdown callback returning while an admitted boost receipt was still suspended inside the isolated purchase store. The profile counters were already clear. A departed player's ledger transaction could still be running at that point.
- Shutdown now closes commerce admission and waits for active ledger transactions with the same 25-second deadline used for profile releases. New and queued work remains pending. An admitted operation can finish or retry within budget, and an already-issued write may still return after the deadline if the server is running. No retries start after that deadline.
- A confirmed commit can acknowledge the receipt without reviving departed or closing cache/UI. An ambiguous commit stays pending. The existing atomic receipt ID and boost expiry allow later replay to confirm the result without granting extra time. Catalog IDs remain disabled and the schema is unchanged.
- Focused commerce lifecycle checks reproduced the old early return. They also covered an admitted write after departure, refused new and queued receipt work, bounded and capped retries, a late confirmed result, and a committed-but-ambiguous result replayed against the same stored receipt. Independent review cleared the shutdown lifecycle.

- An isolated Studio fixture executed the exact old and candidate Commerce and Bootstrap shutdown bodies with the real BoostLedger module and mocked time, receipts and storage. The baseline failed the early-return check. The candidate passed all 78 assertions. The native fixture SHA-256 was `3832d6c5c03fcb20094e445d3e45208d25e4b08481fd8c2c6b8ad717347a4354`, and the disposable session closed. Live Marketplace delivery and storage remain release checks.
- The focused lifecycle suite passed 78 assertions and 17 negative controls, with existing commerce, profile and shutdown checks all passing. Full verification passed 29 pure or synthetic suites, 168 mocked suites, 99 Luau compilations and Rojo builds on unchanged inputs.
- Next reproduce the reported rig gaps and detached shoulder details on the Metronome, Golem and Ignis with matched Studio views before changing their presentation.

## Finish the departure save before admitting a fast rejoin

- Simulated storage reproduced the audit's same-server race. An autosave held the per-user lock with 70 coins, and the final save carrying 91 coins and the rejoin both waited. After the autosave completed, resuming the rejoin first returned temporary progress while the departure save was still queued.
- A final save now reserves the handoff on the exact bound session before waiting. A rejoin waits for either the reservation or the storage lock. Extra release calls and queued autosaves cannot overtake it. The final save enters the same token- and revision-fenced commit path, and the rejoin loads the saved 91 coins under a new writable binding. The old handle remains fenced.
- The reservation clears if the final save reaches its deadline before it can write. Existing deadline, exact-token, confirmed fallback and ambiguous-response recovery paths remain in use. The reproduction covered a departure already queued behind another save. It cannot establish how Roblox orders an actual reconnect ahead of the initial departure callback.

- Studio ran the exact old and new Profiles sources with the real ProfileLease module, isolated fake storage and explicit coroutine ordering. The old code failed the duplicate-release control and the direct rejoin-first control. The candidate passed all 31 checks. The disposable zero-ID place did not use a live DataStore and its Studio session was closed.
- Independent review cleared the handoff ordering. Four focused controls detected bypassed detached-store guard, early rejoin, an overtaking autosave and duplicate release. Profile recovery, cross-server handoff and shutdown races continued to pass. Full verification passed 29 pure or synthetic suites, 168 mocked suites, 99 Luau compilations and Rojo builds on unchanged inputs.
- Next investigate the reported purchase-ledger shutdown race with isolated storage before changing shutdown behavior.

Tests cover late action attributes, separate prompt/art arrivals, real collection changes and resets. ## Show when another player blocks a beam

- The matched Old Stadium replay showed a level-100 beam passing visually through two players even though the nearer player's torso blocked the far defender's damage sight ray. The existing hit resolver already distinguishes those targets.
- When an eligible defender's existing sustained-beam sight ray first hits another player's character, the server sends **BODY BLOCK** privately to that defender. The cue uses the health HUD for up to 1.4 seconds. Hits and dodges take priority, and spawn protection clears the notice. The server sends at most one such notice per second per defender across all attackers. The cue contains no blocker identity.
- The implementation reuses the existing sight ray and keeps damage, reach, and the replicated beam silhouette intact. World geometry, active dodges, and Test Beams do not generate this cue.
- Focused authority and client checks passed 15,482 and 400 assertions respectively, with 57 combined negative controls. Running the new cue regression against the prior source failed as expected. Independent review cleared the damage, protection, and cue-priority paths.

- In the guarded three-client Studio replay, both level-100 casts delivered three beam updates with the same 54.75-stud length. In the offset lane both defenders lost 73 HP and no cover cue appeared. In the centered lane the front player lost 73 HP and the far defender lost zero. Only that defender received a cue and saw **BODY BLOCK**. The 116 × 18 label fit at a 1274 × 671 desktop viewport and was gone by the three-second sample. The tested fixture SHA-256 was `de1b8453d11e95265e20eb9605dace38badde6d72647509d523c92fc73ebdbd1`. All five disposable Studio processes closed.
- The full Map 10 verification passed 29 pure or synthetic suites, 168 mocked-service suites, 99 Luau compilations and Rojo builds. The replay used fixed avatar positions and synthetic aim and power through the normal Action path. Physical-phone readability, real microphones and live multiplayer remain open. The older compact-map integration fixture still expects its original arena boundaries and fails there on the baseline as well. The current Map 10 verification uses its own geometry suites.

## Verify overlapping level-100 attacks in Old Stadium

With the near player centered, their UpperTorso blocked the far target's sight ray. The near defender lost 75 HP and the far defender lost zero. Both casts replicated the same 54.75-stud beam. The clients observed four and three beam updates respectively, which limits the comparison to target eligibility and replicated beam length.
- A single shortened beam would also change the visual cue for other targets with a clear sight ray. Use the matched replay to evaluate a target-specific cover cue before adjusting how players block attacks. [Native evidence](../tests/evidence/stadium-overlap-native-2026-11-22.json)

The candidate drew four quiet paths per beam instead of 77 detailed paths, exposing more of the defender's shoulders and torso while keeping the shafts visible. Shifting both beams away restored full detail. The replay used scripted positions, beams and HUD cues, so human combat readability, live transport and physical-device performance remain open.
- Independent review cleared the rendering change. The focused suite passed 389 assertions and 44 negative controls, including a baseline repro for the off-axis case, a finite endpoint check and an alive-character check. The full run passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 168 mocked-service suites, 99 production compilations and Rojo builds.

## Verify the first Rookie win below full power

- Before recording, the Studio tester used the actual Guide, walked from spawn with keyboard input and turned Beams on. The fixture on `86da0c4` recorded a fresh level-one profile with zero XP and coins at the pad. It supplied normalized power 0.55 and tracked the live Rookie's position for aim. The server observed two cast starts and 56 pulse requests through the Action remote.
- Rookie went from 80 to 37 HP on the first cast and retained that damage through cooldown. The second cast defeated Rookie 13.316 seconds after the trial started. The fixture observed 32 decreases in Rookie's server HP and no reset. The player earned 48 XP and five coins. Three warned return strikes landed for eight damage each while the player stood still, leaving 76 HP.
- Independently, the fixed-target authority fixture predicted two casts and 12.4 simulated seconds at 0.55 with ideal aim and scheduled ticks. The existing authority, combat-quest and training-strike suites passed, including the authority and strike negative controls. The native evidence supports keeping the current Rookie balance for this case.
- This is a local combat and reward check with scripted aim and synthetic input power. It leaves human beam tracking, comfortable voice levels, touch dodging, discovery and live persistence open. The disposable Studio session was closed. The next useful first-session observation is a voice-eligible new player encountering Rookie without a scripted aim or route.

## Keep the defender's hit and dodge feedback truthful

- The previous defender HUD let a dodge replace a recent hit. A second hit could reduce HP during the server's existing one-second warning throttle while the player still saw only the green dodge message. The production helpers reproduced this sequence in isolated Luau, and a staged native UI replay confirmed the rendered green label while the fixture's health decreased.
- The existing defender label now shows **HIT + DODGE** in the damage color when both confirmed cues are current. A dodge that joins an active hit expires with that hit, preventing a misleading green-only tail. A fresh dodge still has its normal cue. A new hit after a dodge keeps its warning after the dodge expires. Combat damage, immunity, server authority and packet rates are untouched.
- The local late-game visual probe staged two level-100 Gold beams. The close frontal view showed both reduced silhouettes and readable health. In a fixed off-axis view, the brighter beam partially covered the avatar's torso while the face and HUD stayed legible. This sampled camera framing with synthetic beams; it does not establish combat readability for real players or physical devices.

- Exact final desktop and iPhone 7 Studio emulator replays covered both hit/dodge orders, the expiry, and a later standalone evade. At 666 × 316 safe pixels the mixed text measured 78 × 13 in a 116 × 18 label and fit beside the staged 375/480 HP. The final replay showed no green-only tail after the staged damage. Real voice, physical input, and live delivery remain open.

- A follow-up replay from an immutable archive of `206d325` exercised the actual Action and Effect remotes in two native Studio clients. Authority reduced defender HP from 100 to 97, accepted Forward Dodge, then reduced HP to 93 and 90 within the first hit's one-second notice throttle. The defender received just one damage packet and one evade packet. At roughly 1274 × 634, the live label showed red HIT + DODGE, fit its 116 × 18 allocation and cleared without a green tail. Positions, readiness, beam power and input timing were scripted locally. No real microphone, physical input, live network or live persistence was exercised.
- Independent review cleared the change. Client integration passed 382 assertions and 41 negative controls. Existing server contact and sustained authority checks passed. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 168 mocked-service suites, 99 production compilations and Rojo builds. Next check current City encounters with an actual eligible voice pair and physical input when an isolated playtest is available.

## Suppress exact duplicate periodic player snapshots

- A native idle client on `0892c88` received 21 periodic `State` packets in 11.516 seconds. Nine exactly duplicated the preceding packet and 11 differed only in the shop clock. The periodic send now compares the whole payload with a copy of the last sent packet. Explicit syncs, action updates, conversations and notifications still send immediately. Changed payloads retain the full schema, including shop timing and quest countdowns. Collected markers keep their periodic refresh for streaming.
- In the matched candidate, the idle client received 11 periodic packets in 11.500 seconds with zero identical neighbors. Ten packets changed only the shop clock. Three explicit `Action` sync requests still received replies even though the payloads matched the preceding packet. This measures packet counts in disposable local Studio. Server comparison costs, bytes on the wire and public-network behavior were outside the run.
- Both fixtures delivered duplicate explicit messages, an urgent save-keyed notice and the subsequent clearing snapshot. A staged Boardwalk Dash timer delivered 6 through 1 and rendered its normal expiry. The combined notice layout temporarily covered the initial seconds of the timer on the candidate, and the remainder was visible. Both disposable Studio sessions closed. No live save or purchase ran.
- The source-derived test reproduced the identical send before the fix. Focused verification passed 223 save/State assertions with 11 negative controls, plus boost status, quest prompt and notification checks. Independent review found no blocker. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 167 mocked-service suites, 98 production compilations and Rojo builds.

## Publish dodge readiness only when the countdown changes

- A single accepted arena Dodge triggered 65 `DodgeReady` changes in about seven seconds on the native baseline, including 28 after the four-second cooldown. The server now sends the original deadline once and updates it on PvP entry only if the normal cooldown extends the premium deadline and is still running. The server still applies the full cooldown on late entry and cancels premium rolls on PvP entry. The client counts down from the published timestamp.
- In the matched candidate, a regular arena dodge produced one server and one client `DodgeReady` change and no updates after expiry. The rendered HUD returned to **DODGE [Q]** at 4.083 seconds versus 4.085 seconds on baseline. The staged idle probe received 15 full `State` deliveries on baseline and 14 on the candidate, each over 8.016 seconds. The full snapshot cadence remains unchanged because it carries time-sensitive quest, commerce, protection and session values.
- A native premium roll crossed into the arena 0.335 seconds after it began. Its deadline changed exactly once from 0.60 to 4.00 seconds, the premium roll ended, and leaving PvP kept the deadline. Entering after the normal deadline sent no new update. Both unpublished Studio fixtures used staged starting positions and the normal local Dodge action. Both closed. This measures attribute changes and HUD behavior in Studio. Physical input, live replication bytes and crowded server timing remain unmeasured.
- Focused verification passed 66 server movement assertions with 13 negative controls, 106 dodge-math checks, 10 cleanup checks and 13 respawn checks. The historical movement fixture's source extraction was repaired and again produces its intended failure. Independent review cleared the deadline and lifecycle behavior. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 167 mocked-service suites, 97 production compilations and Rojo builds.

## Measure four-client beam overlap in populated City

- Exact source `24c182d` ran in a disposable Studio place on one desktop with four local clients, 18 NPCs and 3,023 world instances. Clients staged on Academy safe paving, used synthetic power through normal Action remotes and cast four acknowledged level-100 beams each. The packaged Hollow Purple template was present. All four clients received 253 beam packets each in the active phase and none in idle. This exercised beam replication and client effects on the active map. The safe zone excluded PvP target resolution.
- In paired 30-second idle, beam-cycling and recovery samples, the server saw four simultaneous casts for 14.63 seconds and at least three for 14.80 seconds. Server Heartbeat p95 was 18.06, 18.03 and 18.07 ms across the three phases, and 18.05 ms over 888 samples with at least three active beams. Client frame intervals stayed near the observed 15 Hz Studio cadence. Client p95 across the four windows was 68.05–68.12 ms before, 68.00–68.28 ms during and 67.98–68.16 ms after. Camera and viewport stayed fixed on each client.
- No phase-to-phase interval change was distinguishable at this test's cadence. Frame intervals on one host do not establish GPU time, released-client performance, phone performance or crowded PvP capacity. The benchmark also did not verify visual readability. Continue with target-device profiling and a representative player soak before release. [Native measurements](../tests/evidence/city-overlap-load-2026-11-22.json)
- Argon reviewed the measurement design, and independent review checked the recorded metrics and limitations. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 167 mocked-service suites, 97 production compilations and Rojo builds. All disposable Studio instances closed. No production code changed.

## Require deliberate beam rearm after a protected return

The initial knockout path was invoked directly by the fixture, and the delayed grant was staged rather than measured on a live connection.

## Apply the shutdown deadline to final release writes

- The old shutdown callback stopped waiting after 25 seconds, but a delayed worker could still start its final write afterward. Final releases and departure or late-load releases started during shutdown now receive the same cutoff. Lock waits and retry backoff honor the remaining budget. A request already issued can still settle, and its success or ownership result is recorded. An ambiguous failure remains unconfirmed and uses the existing frozen-snapshot recovery rules.
- The in-memory protocol tests cover late workers, a lock held by an autosave, an in-flight success and failure, and idempotent recovery after a committed response was lost. In isolated Studio with short test deadlines and a fake store, lock expiry started no final write, the already-issued success completed once, and the failed request made no second attempt. The Studio scheduler resumed a lock waiter after its nominal deadline, so the cutoff limits initiation of new requests rather than guaranteeing wall-clock task timing. The disposable Studio closed.
- Independent review cleared the ownership and revision checks. Focused verification passed 53 race/protocol assertions, 13 shutdown callback assertions and five new negative controls. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 167 mocked-service suites, production compilation and review builds. A late acquisition whose release misses the cutoff may leave its lease until expiry. A departure that began before shutdown and an already-started acquisition still use their original retry policy. Live shutdown and rapid-rejoin behavior remain unverified.

## Pace autosave starts instead of launching a server-wide burst

- The previous dispatch loop started all 20 writable profiles on one synthetic 60-second tick. The new scheduler starts the oldest eligible profile at most once per quarter-second. A writable profile first becomes due 30 seconds after admission and is next due 60 seconds after dispatch, including when the profile is quiet and needs its lease renewed.
- A yielding write cannot queue another autosave for the same session. Other profiles can proceed, and deferred work checks that the player, session and saving eligibility are still current before writing. Existing ownership, retry, failure notices, leave saves and final shutdown saves keep their existing policies.
- The isolated Studio-only fixture used the production dispatcher with 20 mock profiles and an adapter that yielded for 0.8 seconds. All 20 completed with no duplicates. Starts occurred over 4.96 seconds, with a minimum 0.250-second gap. That test used Roblox task scheduling without networking or storage services. The disposable Studio closed.
- Independent review found a historical regression fixture still needed the old extraction boundary. Both current and frozen-baseline fixture modes passed after correcting it. Focused tests passed 124 scheduler assertions, 11 autosave-status assertions and four new negative controls. Full verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 167 mocked-service suites, production compilation and review builds. Live DataStore request budgets, retry bursts, shutdown and rapid-rejoin behavior remain release gates. The report's shutdown/rejoin items are separate from this scheduler change.

## Let quiet time during beam cooldown count toward rearm

## Beam animation keeps time at uneven frame rates

- The old renderer discarded fractional frame time after every animation update. Feeding the actual Client gate 31 FPS for ten seconds yielded 155 updates. Keeping the remainder brought the result to the 30 Hz target, with at most one update on each rendered frame and no backlog after stalls. The test covers six fixed frame rates, jitter, long frames and operation below 30 FPS. A reset-to-zero mutation reproduced the defect in both the Client and its Studio benchmark.
- In native desktop Studio at a fixed 666 × 374 emulated viewport, the 24-beam fixture passed 7,829 checks and cleaned up. Regular, crowded and reduced modes each rendered 151 updates at about 30.1 Hz. Script-section p95 was 4.00 ms, 1.12 ms and 0.47 ms respectively. Median frame intervals were about 16.7 ms in all phases. The first two attempts aborted on viewport drift. See [native benchmark results](../tests/evidence/beam-cadence-native-2026-11-21.json) for the completed run.
- Independent review and the full local verification passed 29 pure/synthetic suites, 164 mocked-service suites, production compilation and review builds. The native scene used synthetic overlapping beam visuals and measured script duration on this PC. Physical-phone, GPU and live multiplayer performance remain open. The viewport was restored and the disposable Studio session closed.

## Combat HUD shows beam phases and stamina at a glance

The updated control uses slate when OFF, amber when armed, green while firing and cool slate during cooldown. Firing and labeled cooldown text stays on the button when the full meter is visible. The existing attack gates also control whether the face looks ready, and the timer rails do not intercept input.
- With simulated microphone power and normal client/server combat, the 567 × 242 HUD showed all four faces with fitting text inside the actual 260 × 44 button. The shot reduced Rookie's health from 80 to 74. The 750 × 323 follow-up repeated the label and timer checks; its staged shot missed. Under normal desktop sprint keys and movement, server stamina fell from 10 to 9.47, the new rail read 94.66%, and stamina refilled to 10 after release. All 312 local checks and builds passed with matching inputs. The disposable sessions closed. Next, test the redesigned controls through a fresh session on a physical phone.

## Beam templates skip repeated settings writes in the city

- The beam renderer now updates template visibility and motion on mode changes instead of assigning the same values on every animation update. Particle rates are capped when the template initializes, and transitions still clear hidden particles once. A 24-beam Hollow Purple overlap can avoid up to 432 unchanged property writes per steady animation update. Building, moving, resizing and rendering the beams use the existing paths.
- In a fixed Academy scene with 1,912 streamed city descendants, both native runs showed 192 pooled parts, 51 enabled template beams and four enabled emitters while crowded. Regular mode showed 496 parts, 120 template beams and 96 emitters in both. Both paths released all allocations. Frame medians remained about 67 ms, and animation p95 moved from 4.687 to 4.483 ms in the regular control and from 1.279 to 1.341 ms in the first crowded phase. The samples show no clear timing improvement. They used one temporary player and staged visuals on desktop Studio. [Measurements](../tests/evidence/beam-performance-populated-2026-11-21.json)
- Critic cleared the implementation. Focused tests passed 7,273 template assertions, eight negative controls and 115,412 pool assertions. All 312 local checks and builds passed with matching inputs. The disposable sessions closed. Next, profile the live city on physical hardware during representative multiplayer overlap.

The regression failed when that flag was removed again, and all 312 local checks and builds passed with matching input hashes. Disposable Studio sessions closed. Next, measure larger rendering costs on a representative device and continue current-world polish before the separate PvP arena.

- Removed target lock, acquired-target marker and touch/R/R3 lock controls. Mouse free aim follows the cursor, and touch and gamepad free aim share the centered reticle using Roblox preferred input. Native Studio showed the touch reticle and the absence of lock UI. Active gamepad hardware remains unverified.
- The idle HUD has one MENU launcher that toggles the existing Shop, Beams, Quests, Style, Map and Settings group. A quest claim or a notice that cannot fit may expose a temporary shortcut. A 567 × 241 native touch-emulator run opened the group via MENU and closed it with B. Minimap and core combat controls keep their existing access.
- Enabled server collision on 74 authored Map 10 finishing pieces, including gate stonework, benches, planters, bollards and palm trunks. Cosmetic pieces remain non-solid. Before/after native walk probes with jumping and climbing disabled crossed the bench and gate stone in the baseline and stopped at their surfaces afterward. The same walk through the open gate still reached the other side. Three additional solid examples passed matched ray probes. This checks selected props and corridors, not traversal of every map object.
- All 312 local commands and builds passed with matching input hashes. The retired lock suites were replaced by free-aim checks and the standalone historical review builders were updated. Next visual pass: redesign the combat HUD and grouped menu around clear combat, progress and navigation priorities. Compare desktop and short mobile states in Studio before expanding the redesign.

## One profile from opening to first gear

- Locked Garden and Beach quest cards now offer **GUIDE TO MAR** in the City. The 44 px control uses the existing Gardens route and leaves quest discovery to Talk. The original MEET NPC button activated in Studio but left the menu open and selected no destination.
- On a fresh temporary profile in the iPhone 17 Pro landscape emulator (750 × 323 safe HUD), the tool walked from spawn to Rookie. Synthetic mic power and aim through the normal client/server path reached level 2 and 10 coins in four casts. The garden button fit, closed the menu and drew Gardens breadcrumbs. The tool walked to Mar, opened Talk and accepted the quest. The fixture then staged the avatar near each marker and used normal keyboard prompts. Both claims used the actual menu buttons. The in-game coin ledger was 10 → 90 → 140 → 40 after buying and equipping the 100-coin Megaphone. The model remounted after same-session respawn and the multiplier stayed 1.06.
- Critic and all 314 local commands passed. This is one continuous local profile with automated input and staged marker travel. It does not establish human discovery, real microphone or physical touch, travel time, or live persistence. Next: retain this route while simplifying the HUD and testing visible-object collision and free aim.

## Voice guidance on short phone screens

- In the 567 × 241 safe HUD, the original BEAMS button showed generic toggle copy even with voice denied, while connecting, during cooldown and after ready. Taller layouts retain the full meter and ordinary toggle label. The legacy hold-to-beam branch and server combat are unchanged.
- Matched native Studio captures show the cooldown countdown and quiet instruction. All 11 staged states fit at 241 safe pixels, with the longest text measuring 116 × 24. The short cue also rendered at 242 and 313 pixels, and the full meter returned at 315. The earlier tester layout placed its 44 px launcher at y14 across the upper 12 px of BEAMS. The rebuilt tester launcher sits at y2–46 and BEAMS begins at y46. Automated pointer input on BEAMS switched it OFF. The tester clearance check covers safe heights from 239 pixels. These are Studio emulator checks with synthetic state and input, without live voice or phone hardware.
- Critic reran 240 voice presentation assertions and controls, the production contact integration regression, and the minimap sweep across short tester widths. The full aggregate passed all 314 commands with unchanged input hashes. Next: capture a continuous first-session run from spawn to first earned equipment with human voice and a physical phone, and measure live combat readability before advancing to the dedicated PvP world.

## Defender feedback for a beam hit

- The health card now displays **BEAM HIT YOU** for 1.4 seconds after the first nonfatal hit from an opposing sustained cast. The server sends the cue privately to the damaged player after actual health loss. It binds to the current Humanoid and clears on character removal or spawn protection. Subsequent ticks from the same cast leave the timer alone. The existing knockout notice and the attacker's damage readout remain separate. The cue uses steady text and works with Beams OFF.
- In matched native two-client Studio captures, the same synthetic server-side strike reduced the defender from 100 to 97 HP. The baseline showed only the health change. The candidate displayed the label beside 97/100 with Beams OFF. Native text bounds were 88 × 13 within a 116 × 18 lane. The defender received three confirmations for three damaging cases, and the attacker received none of the defender event. All eight boundary cases passed. The cue was hidden again after the hold. The fixture stages players and power in an unpublished local place. Human voice, phone and live persistence remain unverified.
- Independent Critic reran 148 server and 330 client integration assertions with 32 negative controls. The full local verifier passed 314 commands after updating the voice tests' callback locators for the shared respawn handler. Next: test the opening through first equipment with a real microphone, test phone controls and assess defender readability during consecutive casts.

## Converging beams from the far detail band

- Three level-100 segments of 50, 20 and 53 studs converged at the same point 60 studs from the viewer. The third origin was 113 studs from the viewer. Origin-only crowding left 77 parts on the nearer remote beam. Counting the closest point on the finite segment now uses five parts for that remote beam, with the local beam at 77 parts and the far beam at four in both runs.
- The matched unpublished Studio probe used the actual Roblox renderer module and real Parts in the local server DataModel, with the candidate loaded as a disposable module. Both pools cleared. It did not measure screenshots or client GPU cost. A separate native server fixture passed all eight PvP boundary cases with two local clients and a synthetic power tick. Three valid cases removed three HP on the server, and the other five caused zero damage. Temporary client observers each received all eight beam and end packets. The attacker received three hit confirmations and the defender received zero. The synchronous fixture restored health before a client health decrement was observed, so it does not establish defender HUD readability or human voice behavior.
- Independent Critic passed 320 client integration assertions and 20 negative controls plus 115,412 pool assertions and seven negative controls. The full verifier passed all 314 commands with matching input hashes. Integration tests cover both directions, finite length and camera culling. Next: capture matched defender-view combat and test human voice/physical devices.

## Overlapping beam audio

- An unloaded remote clip previously halved a loaded remote clip's normal gain from .40 to .20 before group gain and spatial rolloff. The adapter now admits only loaded active clips and previously started fades to the shared gain calculation. Clips canceled before first playback stay silent and consume no gain. The four-handle limit, voice ceiling and calibration mute still apply.
- Independent review passed 29 adapter, 481 level, 2,148 LOW baseline and 344 cadence assertions plus 12 negative controls. The full local test and build matrix passed. These are simulated readiness and gain checks, with real asset playback, voice audibility and physical-device mix still open.

## Target visibility during overlapping beams

- When three sustained beam origins are within the existing 110-stud detail radius, remote beams drop secondary collars, strands, sparks, template ribbons and particles. Remote core dimensions and motion stay the same. The local regular beam and local Test Beam retain their detail. Remote endpoint lenses fade during overlap and a confirmed hit briefly fades its own endpoint lens so the hit strokes can show through.
- Matched native captures in the current City stadium used six level-100 beams, the actual Hollow Purple template, the same camera and a staged hit anchor. The target outline and 16 hit strokes became visible as active pooled beam pieces fell from 406 to 102, enabled template Beams from 30 to 15, and emitters from 24 to four. The client integration and template doubles also cover restoration after a beam ends, the 0.28-second hit expires, the detailed range changes, and reduced effects take precedence.
- The exact 24-beam local Studio benchmark recorded 496 regular versus 192 crowded active pooled beam pieces, 120 versus 51 enabled template Beams and 96 versus four emitters. The measured script-section p95 fell from 5.63 ms to 2.05 ms; all phases stayed near the same 67 ms frame interval. These are synthetic native rendering measurements, not GPU, production multiplayer, or physical-phone performance. Independent Critic cleared the change.
- Next: run a native multi-client combat/readability pass with a defender’s view and collect real microphone and physical-device evidence. Finish the current-world pass before the sound-themed PvP world and return portal.

## Confirmed hits in overlapping beams

- Six nearby level-100 beams exhausted secondary geometry on the previous native renderer. A valid simulated contact was accepted but allocated zero strokes. The renderer now reserves 16 of the 512 pooled Parts for the local confirmed-hit effect and limits secondary geometry to 376. All 24 admitted beams still have five core slots each. On matched local Studio builds with the actual Hollow Purple template, contact allocated 0 strokes before and 16 after. The expiry and teardown probe returned the slots and cleared the pool. The matched captures still show dense visual overlap at the target, so allocation alone does not establish readability.
- The native performance fixture now uses the production Hollow Purple asset, converging paths, and per-rig core/particle checks. For 24 beams it recorded 496 active pooled slots, 120 enabled template Beams and 96 enabled emitters in regular mode, versus 96 slots, 48 Beams and zero emitters in reduced mode. The instrumented script-section p95 was 6.03 ms regular and 1.09 ms reduced. Frame intervals stayed near the same roughly 67 ms cadence across all three phases, so this is not an FPS improvement or GPU result. Template anchors are outside the pooled Part count.
- Independent review confirmed allocation limits and prompted the per-rig checks. The composition suite passed 115,412 assertions and seven negative controls, including removal of the hit reserve. Next: tune overlapping secondary effects against a native arena view, then verify actual player readability and performance on a physical phone. The dedicated sound-themed PvP world and training return portal still follow the current-world pass.

## Beam, player health and boss pacing (unpublished balance candidate)

- Smoothed mid-tier sustained damage and raised mid-/late-player HP. Same-level and ±5 matchups retain a two-cast window under synthetic full-power contact. Lv100 vs Lv1 still reaches the existing 1.0s full-power endpoint on the actual mocked server path. Keep the 4s maximum / 5s cooldown, power gate, gear and PvP boundaries.
- Lowered boss health for unlock-level solo fights and separated ranged vs melee damage. See [actual-protocol pacing scenarios](BOSS_COMBAT_BALANCE_REVIEW.md). Model results estimate 30–40s at sustained full contact and 52–63s for the defined imperfect/dodge schedule. Exact solo Studio passed 10/10 boss retaliation checks; isolated real-server beam budget runs verified the two endpoint bosses. Human/mobile/multiplayer encounter balance and live release are not claimed.

## Four-way dodge and themed bosses (local review)

- Q or touch Dodge uses a direction from keyboard or current movement. The server maps it to one of four avatar-relative directions while preserving facing, collision checks, duration and cooldown. No tumble.
- Boss appearances scale 1.4× from the floor with the same model part budgets. The center-body aim samples cover their taller silhouettes, but cannot extend ray length or bypass cover.
- Five encounters have distinct patterns with readable warnings: pendulum bars, seismic slam/faults, flame lanes, sonar/undertow and staggered bell disks. A single shape definition drives both warnings and damage.

## November 16 four-way dodge extension

## November 10 recovery admission and autosave notice correction (historical; unsafe lifetime acknowledgement superseded above)

Atomic exact-token checks reject newer same-job/foreign owners without altering their data or lease expiry; lost successful acquisition responses acknowledge the same token without restamping. No temporary/stale handle becomes writable. Clear admission after success or confirmed foreign rejection.

## November 10 approved bounded recovery (initial checkpoint; admission correction above)

- Implemented the owner's last-confirmed-save fallback choice. On same-server rejoin after a failed final release, retry the detached final snapshot at most three additional times, with a 12-second cutoff for starting further recovery calls, then read/acquire the latest stored checkpoint. Existing save retries and shutdown deadline are unchanged. Another active acquisition is never stolen; storage/ownership failures yield temporary unsavable data, never writable defaults.
- Each acquisition has a unique token; saves have monotonic revisions and profile-table bindings. Exact committed-operation recognition handles lost responses without rewriting an old snapshot or restamping a newer lease. Replayed callbacks recheck the current token/revision and cannot overwrite a later protocol save. Developer baseline copies preserve their legitimate binding.
- Fallback warns that recent progress may be missing, with persistent desktop and compact/mobile status. A review correction preserves observed confirmed data after ambiguous acquisition responses, and another adds the missing narrow-screen warning.
- Validation: 53 recovery checks, seven targeted negative controls, 24 pure/synthetic suites, 55 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all builds pass. UI-only native fixture passes 33 cases, including six warning states. Separate self-review completed; no separate reviewer claimed. [Exact evidence](../tests/evidence/bounded-recovery-2026-11-10.json) and [policy/invariants](PROFILE_RECOVERY_REVIEW.md).
- Limits: recovery occurs on rejoin, retained snapshots are memory-only, and an already in-flight engine request can outlast the deadline. Unknown acquisition outcomes remain read-only until safe acquisition/expiry. Simulations do not prove real DataStore contention/outages/shutdown; physical devices and mixed-version release migration remain gates. No live DataStore writes, game publication or audio configuration. Next: independent code review and an owner-authorized isolated persistence test before any release.

## November 10 failed-departure follow-up (historical; recovery policy superseded above)

- Reconciled shared head `6014930` and corrected README controls to SCREAMS ON/OFF, including talk-without-attacking, respawn and session-local behavior.
- Reproduced ordinary departure ignoring `Save` returning `false, reason` and exceptions omitting UserId. The narrow correction now logs returned failure or exception with UserId/reason, consistently with late-load/shutdown callers. No retry/rejoin policy, lease ownership, shutdown budget or saved-data behavior changed.
- Re-ran recovery with actual Profiles/ProfileLease and ordinary departure over simulated persistence: failed release makes three attempts, leaves the last durable 100 coins versus 150 unsaved and blocks same-server rejoin; another owner loads 100 at expiry and stale writes cannot replace its newer 200. A committed release with a response error returns ownership failure despite durable 90 coins. Logs therefore do not establish whether data committed or a lease was released.
- Passed 30 recovery checks, 23 departure checks (including delayed failures), four targeted negative controls, 24 pure/synthetic suites, 53 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all builds. Separate self-review found no blocker in the reporting correction. [Exact evidence](../tests/evidence/departure-release-2026-11-10.json).
- Strongest unresolved blocker: no terminal-release recovery after detachment; same-server stale ownership can deny saving indefinitely and foreign takeover exposes rollback to the last checkpoint. Recommend bounded immutable-snapshot retry only while atomic ownership permits, then latest durable checkpoint with explicit rollback notice if ownership moves. Retry/admission duration and fallback UX need an owner decision before implementation; no automatic merge or stale overwrite. [Recovery recommendation](PROFILE_RECOVERY_REVIEW.md). No native run, live DataStore writes, publication or audio configuration in this follow-up.

## Product direction

The loop is: aim, scream into the mic, fight for space, earn XP and unlock more destructive screams. The city should create interesting combat routes, cover and breakable opportunities. Side activities support that loop. Build fair, readable, responsive combat before paid products or cosmetic breadth.

- The current chat's scheduled development follow-up was removed at the owner's request on November 9. Continue work on request; do not recreate scheduled work without a new request. Earlier scheduling entries below are historical.
- Maintain completed evidence, known failures and the next concrete action here. Actual audio, hardware and multiplayer gates stay open until observed.
