## November 10 transport assertion correction

- Review of `161590f` identified test masking risks, not confirmed production failures: an inactive beam after three seconds could be input timeout, and later valid pulses could conceal a replay mutation. The earlier transport run remains historical evidence; these two claims now use stricter assertions.
- The disposable server fixture records immutable state and return values inside each actual Begin/Pulse/Stop call without yielding. Five replay/malformed requests must explicitly reject and preserve the captured state. Quiet-stop requires one matching client send, an accepted server stop with reason `stop`, and a timestamp before both expiry deadlines.
- Final native baseline passes 40 assertions in 30.869 seconds. Explicit stop precedes input timeout by 0.250790 seconds and maximum duration by 3.483515 seconds. The dropped-stop negative control fails at the missing explicit-stop assertion (31.354 seconds); the corrupt-replay control fails at immediate power mutation (5.191 seconds), preserving evidence of power changing from 0.5 to 1 despite a rejection. Both are expected failures proving detection, not production defects.
- Full `python tests/verify_local.py --build` passes 24 pure/synthetic suites, 52 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all candidate/disposable builds; both mutation artifacts also compile. Complete reports survive Studio log truncation through bounded chunks. [Exact results, snapshots and build hashes](../tests/evidence/transport-assertions-2026-11-10.json); [reproduction and mutation commands](VOICE_TRANSPORT_REVIEW.md).
- Separate implementing-agent source/diff review completed; no independent reviewer claimed for this correction. Production sources are unchanged. Microphone/aim/focus are synthetic; real microphone, phone/input, internet conditions, multiplayer contention, live saves and audio mix remain unverified. No publication or audio/product configuration. Next: independent review of this correction, then eligible real-device first-minute and two-account playtests.

## November 10 shutdown reward persistence fix

- Inspected server combat validation/credit, progression rewards, profile lease/load/save and lifecycle callers from `375c816`. Reproduced a production shutdown race before editing behavior: the actual remote handler accepted a completed quest during the final save's yield, displaying 50 coins/100 XP and marking it claimed, while the save retained 10 coins/0 XP and an unclaimed quest. This is deterministic mocked-store evidence of lost accepted progress, not an observed live player incident.
- Shutdown now closes admission and detaches all gameplay sessions/cancels attacks before starting any final save. Existing reward helpers and remote handlers reject further mutations through their session checks. Tracked profile acquisitions finishing during close release their lease instead of reopening gameplay; shutdown waits for them within its existing 25-second budget. Returned final-save failures now warn as well as exceptions. Pre-close rewards and developer baselines retain the existing save path.
- Full `python tests/verify_local.py --build` passes 24 pure/synthetic suites, 53 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all builds. Focused coverage: 15 actual shutdown/reward/join/Profiles race checks and 11 shutdown lifecycle checks. Regressions include final-snapshot timing, rejection without losing claimability, pre-close persistence/reload, delayed acquisition/release, exceptions, non-saving sessions and bounded waits. Removing session detachment or join waiting in temporary negative controls fails the intended assertions.
- Separate implementing-agent source/diff review completed; no independent reviewer claimed. [Exact reproduction, checks and build hashes](../tests/evidence/shutdown-rewards-2026-11-10.json). No Studio/native or live DataStore execution this checkpoint; no publication or audio configuration.
- Next: independent review, then an isolated real DataStore shutdown/leave/rejoin and competing-server lease test before release. Store outages beyond retries and the engine shutdown deadline can still lose unsaved progress; this fix prevents accepting new progress after final saves begin, not guaranteed persistence under outages. Real microphone/phone/multiplayer gates also remain open.

## November 10 late profile release failure reporting

- Independent review of `2ce082b` identified an ignored result in the late-load path. Reproduced before changing production code: actual `Profiles.Save` exhausted three injected write failures and returned `false, "retry"`; the lease remained present, but Bootstrap produced no final release-failure warning. The earlier successful-release regression did not cover this outcome.
- Late loads finishing after shutdown or player departure now check both `pcall` completion and the Save result, logging a consistent late-release failure with UserId and the returned reason or exception. They remain outside gameplay; tracked work completes only after the release call returns. This attempts release and reports failure; it does not guarantee lease release or add recovery retries beyond the existing Profiles policy.
- Full checks/build pass: 24 pure/synthetic suites, 53 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all builds. The expanded actual shutdown/reward/join/Profiles regression passes 34 checks; shutdown lifecycle and profile concurrency retain 11 and 13 passing checks. Delayed release is held inside UpdateAsync with the lease still present and shutdown still waiting; success is checked only after the write resumes. Failed releases in both closing/departed paths preserve the lease and emit one final warning after three attempts. Exceptions use the same warning path.
- Three temporary negative controls fail as intended: ignore returned failure, omit failure reason, and launch an untracked release. Separate post-implementation self-review completed; no external reviewer claimed. [Evidence and build identity](../tests/evidence/late-release-2026-11-10.json).
- Limits/next: failed writes can still leave a lease held. Native engine shutdown timing, live DataStore contention/outage/rejoin and competing-server lease validation remain required before release. No live DataStore writes, Studio run, publication or audio/product configuration this checkpoint.

## November 10 failed-release recovery investigation

- From `39beb40`, reproduced three exhausted release attempts (waits 1/2/3 seconds), retained local ownership, same-server rejoin rejection without datastore access even after lease expiry and another simulated hour, and foreign-owner acquisition at expiry using only last durable progress. A stale write remains correctly rejected after another owner saves. An ambiguous post-commit error can instead return false/ownership even though release and progress were committed.
- No automatic recovery change: clearing the local marker can abandon unsaved progress; writing a retained snapshot after ownership changes can corrupt newer data. The actual Profiles diagnostic passes 21 checks; deleting either atomic ownership guard fails its negative control. Full existing verification/build passes 24 pure/synthetic suites, 53 mocked-service suites, 56 production compiles and all builds. Production sources are unchanged.
- [Review and owner decision](PROFILE_RECOVERY_REVIEW.md), [exact evidence](../tests/evidence/profile-recovery-2026-11-10.json). Recommend bounded, ownership-checked retry with an immutable retained final snapshot; after ownership moves, never replay stale data. Owner decision needed: latest durable checkpoint with an explicit rollback notice (recommended), or blocked progression pending recovery/support. Specify retry duration and player messaging before implementation. A durable journal is a separate design.
- Separate self-review completed. Simulated owners/store/clock only; no native Studio or live datastore activity. Real contention/outage/rejoin and engine deadlines remain release gates. No publication or audio/product configuration.

## Ordered backlog

### P0 — combat progression redesign (implemented locally; human balance testing next)

- Three west-beach training tiers, server-owned telegraphed retaliation, level-band rewards, shared KO/assist credit, stronger repeat-opponent limits, three persistent renewing combat challenges and ten-level milestone/stat previews. Map marker 8 identifies the training pads.
- XP curve and rewards tuned together. Deterministic steady solo-training scenario estimates level 10 in 10.8 minutes, 25 in 62.4 minutes, 50 in 3.9 hours and 100 in 15.9 hours; these are assumptions, not measured playtime. See [balance specification](PROGRESSION_BALANCE.md).
- Follow-up bug/optimization review: reproduced and fixed missing/expired damage becoming a full last-hit payout; combined KO/challenge notices to preserve completion feedback and reduce one recipient's double-challenge payout from three reward state sends to one (excluding level-up messages). No frame-rate gain is claimed.
- Automated verification/build: 20 pure/synthetic suites, 48 mocked-service suites, 46 production scripts. Native fixture and build identity are recorded in [evidence](../tests/evidence/progression-redesign-2026-11-09.json). Real voice, multiplayer, physical-phone layouts, live saves and actual pacing remain open.
- Next: owner/friend tests at levels 1/25/60/100; measure deaths, KOs/minute, assist credit and progress per session. GitHub development sync authorized; Roblox publication still requires approval. No schedule created.

### P0 — progression foundation (implemented locally; native review pending)

- Cap progression/combat at level 100, with a base scream at level 1 and unlocks at 10, 20, …, 100.
- Keep level-specific damage/range/health bounded; preserve old progress/style data when loading 300-level saves.
- Verify unlock boundaries, overflow XP, retired styles, idempotent migration and reload. Produce a local build; do not publish.
- Why first: continuous damage and destruction need a coherent level/balance model, and a cap change must not erase progress.

### P0 — voice-triggered continuous combat (local client/server prototype wired; beam feedback wired; native verification pending)

- Replace hold/release as the default attack trigger. Sustained calibrated voice starts an attack, aiming tracks during the scream, quiet/mute stops it.
- Prototype 4 seconds maximum active time, then 10 seconds cooldown from attack end. Compare with shorter active windows in timed playtests; keep tuning data-driven.
- Use an explicit armed/paused state and clear indicator so calibration, menus, NPC dialogue, typing, spawn protection, rolls, knockdown, death and lost focus cannot attack. Ordinary conversation/background noise is a detection limitation; test a calibrated onset threshold/hysteresis and clear local arm/pause control without requiring E per attack.
- Server owns start/end deadlines, tick rate, damage budget, range/cover/PvP and reward limits. One sustained scream must not become repeated full-damage casts, per-tick XP farming or unlimited destruction. Reject stale/replayed pulses, missing stop messages and aim/power abuse; use server expiry even if the client disappears.
- Rework beam effects as one updated bounded effect per attacker rather than creating an entire old burst every tick. Rework feedback, mic controller and server acknowledgement together.
- Re-test mouse/first-person/touch aim, rolling interruption, short noises, noise during cooldown, reconnect, respawn and silence. Prototype locally before requesting real-mic friend tests.
- Pure modules and integration contract: [SUSTAINED_ATTACK_PROTOCOL.md](SUSTAINED_ATTACK_PROTOCOL.md). The disposable local prototype has client/server wiring, but native Studio and real microphones are unverified.

### P1 — destruction as combat design

- Existing destructibles: bins/benches, trees/bikes and approved building facades. Auto-repair and occupied-repair guards exist.
- Retune health/time-to-break against sustained damage and the 100-level cap. Level unlocks should change tactics, not permit unrestricted whole-map collapse.
- Put useful breakable cover/flanking routes in the arena. Preserve road surfaces, essential buildings, spawn and quest dependencies.
- Verify replication, late joins, occupied repairs, NPC recovery and memory/effect budgets in multiplayer.

### P1 — PvP readability and fair progression

- Keep optional first person, rolls, sprint and visible central arena boundaries.
- Measure time-to-knockout at low/mid/high levels, new-player survivability and damage-per-active-second versus the full attack/cooldown cycle.
- Add readable enemy health/hit confirmation, clear beam/cover feedback and concise combat onboarding where needed.
- Review rewards so player combat/objectives outweigh farming harmless workers. Recommend separating training/fighter targets from occupational residents; implement after reward/quest impact review.
- Consider a simple arena objective (capture/hold a plaza or moving contested zone) only after basic combat is enjoyable; avoid adding multiple modes immediately.

### P1 — bugs, performance and saves

- Inspect the version-15 cloud/source difference before any integration; do not overwrite collaborator edits.
- Reproduce reported failures, add focused regression coverage and measure before optimization.
- Profile server startup/streaming, NPC pathfinding, combat effects and destruction under representative attack/player load. The ~50k-part city and prior early render stall are risk indicators, not proven bottlenecks.
- Real test-universe migration/save contention/rejoin; physical desktop/phone controls and 30-minute multiplayer soak remain required.

### P2 — city supporting the fight

- Keep Barcelona identity, beach, landmark sightlines, authored courtyards and purposeful worker activity.
- Favor denser playable routes and cover over increasing the empty map footprint or NPC count. Limit work to representative blocks/arena spaces and compare street-height before/after views.
- De-emphasize delivery/gardening UI during PvP. Keep Nick as an optional discovery; do not expand side-quest systems before core combat.

### Owner-requested shop, UI and beam effects — implemented locally

- The owner explicitly advanced this work on November 9: one permanent pass for every beam color, separate 30-minute XP/coin boosts that continue offline after activation, a redesigned shop/HUD and stronger VFX at higher levels.
- Spectrum HSV/hex color mixer, existing free colors, Freerunner safe-area movement, inventory boost activation and server-owned wall-clock multipliers are wired. Unconfigured products display Coming soon. Atomic purchase records are separate from gameplay profiles; receipts and activation retries are idempotent.
- Animated sustained beam cores, traveling rings, one/two spiral strands and endpoint coronas scale with server attack level. Part pooling, 24 attackers, 512-part global cap, core reservation, reduced-effects and distance limits bound presentation. Damage/timing/audio remain unchanged.
- Local verification: 22 pure/synthetic suites, 50 mocked-service suites, 52 production compiles and rebuilt candidate/disposable places. Native Studio: 587 beam geometry/pool assertions, 35 integrated damage/destruction/progression checks and 21 UI layout cases passed. Evidence and remaining limits are in [shop/beam evidence](../tests/evidence/shop-beams-2026-11-09.json).
- [Product configuration, behavior and release checks](SHOP_AND_BEAMS.md). Next: owner reviews local game/visual previews; create/approve product IDs and prices, then use a separate approved test experience to validate actual receipts, pass ownership, saves/rejoins and regional prices before live sales. Physical-phone, real-mic and multiplayer performance checks remain open. No Roblox publication or schedule.

## Review findings and recommendations

- **Change:** old hold-E/release flow is mechanically awkward for a microphone game. Continuous voice should be immediate and visibly armed, with bounded server simulation.
- **Change:** old 300-level / 5-level style cadence is spread across Config, Progression, VoiceCombat, UI and profile normalization. A one-constant change would leave inconsistent caps and saves.
- **Keep/refine:** level-gated facade/prop destruction, timed repair, cover and central PvP support the concept. Existing essential-route protection is valuable.
- **Reduce priority:** civilian farming, routine delivery/gardening and cosmetic breadth compete with PvP development. Retain working content while testing a clearer combat reward loop.
- **Performance risk:** blindly firing the existing burst effect and reward handler every frame would multiply VFX, remote traffic, NPC rewards and destruction. Build a dedicated sustained-attack budget.
- **Balance risk:** four seconds active plus ten seconds off is a 14-second full cycle. Test encounter pacing and positioning; do not simply apply four seconds of current per-cast damage every update.
- **Validation gaps:** source/unit/Studio passes do not establish live voice audibility, real-device comfort, saved-data safety or representative multiplayer load.

- NPC route/pose refinement: ignore path-block notifications behind the active waypoint; timed-out waypoints now abort and stop instead of skipping ahead through obstacles. - City color refinement: refined frontages now use district-specific palettes with deterministic per-building shade variation rather than the same four paints throughout the entire city. - Automatic voice HUD: added a server-derived active-attack deadline so players see remaining speaking time, not just a generic attacking label; cleared on end and initial join. - Added AutomaticVoiceFlow.luau: nine simulated calibrated-RMS → onset → client transport → server adapter/damage checks pass, including background suppression, four-second stop/budget, no restart from voice held throughout cooldown, fresh quiet rearm, UI cancellation, missing-pulse expiry and mic reconnect pause. This uses synchronous fake transport/scalar geometry, not real voice/network. Updated native first-person fixture to test camera-switch cancellation in either combat mode; compile only. Reviewed aiming against Roblox official laser tutorial; existing GetMouseLocation/ViewportPointToRay pairing is correct and unchanged.

- Sustained-destruction scheduling: replaced one delayed repair task per accepted hit with one pending repair timer per object that follows the latest hit deadline, retries occupied repairs after five seconds and exits when object is removed. Maintenance can finish while timer waits without restoring twice. Eight injected-clock tests pass: 600 hits produced two total wakeups and at most one pending callback (synthetic scheduling evidence, not measured FPS/server time). Destruction compiles and candidate builds; native occupied-repair/maintenance regression remains pending.

- Aim lifecycle hardening: camera-ray lookup now returns no aim while CurrentCamera is absent or its viewport is not initialized; both automatic and legacy callers cancel/skip rather than indexing a missing ray. This protects spawn/camera replacement transitions. Client compilation and automatic control/flow checks pass; engine camera-replacement reproduction remains pending.

- Added local resident secondary animation: subtle footfall body motion, idle breathing/glances, reading head tilt and work lean. These reuse existing custom R6 root/neck joints and distance-based update frequency, with no new replicated instances or per-frame server messages. Residents compiles and candidate rebuilds; appearance/rig blending still awaits native visual review.

- Scheduled continuation updated in place (existing active four-hour heartbeat): recognizes completed version-19 release, unpublished automatic candidate, pending manual tests and permission to continue independent work. No duplicate automation or background process installed. All current work pushed; generated candidate and disposable fixtures rebuilt from current source.

- Immediate continuation requested by owner (not waiting for scheduled heartbeat). Added 16 actual Destruction service checks with mocked engine objects/scheduler: restores collision/seat/prompt originals, defers occupied repair, respects subsequent-hit deadlines, removal and maintenance interactions. Added server-confirmed cumulative damage feedback for sustained attacks; counts only applied damage (not blocked/level-rejected hits), rounds fractional finishing health for display and sends one update per damaging tick to the attacker. Fourteen pure presentation checks cover expiry, duplicates, out-of-order totals/sequences and invalid data. Scripts compile and candidate builds; native HUD review pending.

- Escape-menu interruption fix: Roblox CoreGui menu now blocks attack/input, immediately disarms voice and stops sprint; closing it requires explicit resume and fresh quiet. Shared focus/menu suspension path avoids divergent behavior. Five added simulated mic-to-server interruption checks pass (14 total); client compiles. Real CoreGui event/device verification is pending in the manual checklist.

- Added 70 deterministic sustained-balance checks using actual attack/stats/destruction rules with perfect aim and synthetic pulses. Representative full-power player matchups require 2–5 attacks and overcome current quiet healing; destructible tiers take 1–5 full attacks at unlock and remain level-gated. Important tuning concern: level 1 attacking level 100 at onset-strength power (.35) took 16 attacks / 218.8 seconds with current 20%-per-ten-quiet-seconds healing. These are simulation assumptions, not playtest times; do not call the 4s/10s cadence balanced yet. Next balance review should compare comfortable calibrated power, mixed-level matches and healing/cooldown together before changing live tuning.

- NPC grounding recovery now invalidates the old route and clears working/audience/seat state before returning an airborne resident to its last grounded position. Previously the still-running route coroutine could resume toward its pre-recovery waypoint. World compiles; candidate and both disposable fixtures rebuild. Native forced-fall/route-recovery observation remains pending; no claim of an executed physics test.

- Verification workflow repair: reproduced legacy UI harness build failure from unsupported requires and stale module lists. Bundler now resolves repository literal dependencies transitively, rejecting unknown/circular/ambiguous modules; UI fake remotes advertise automatic mode. This command performs no Studio execution or publication. README now documents the unified check.

- Collection/control readability: scream cards now show automatic-mode DPS, maximum duration and actual cooldown rather than legacy burst damage/4s cooldown. Shared DamageScale drives both adapter and readout (no tuning change); two-line cards have more vertical space, collection subtitle resets after visiting other tabs, and touch dodge/sprint labels omit keyboard keys. Unified local verification/build passes; phone/desktop visual fit still pending.

- Camera/menu UI lifecycle: resident label selection now handles missing CurrentCamera by clearing labels/target instead of calling a method on nil during camera replacement. - November 9 combat HUD: health now follows the replicated character Humanoid instead of waiting for periodic full-state packets. Also removed a competing collection subtitle assignment that alternated text on state refresh. Next: native damage/respawn HUD check and continued combat-state review.

- November 9 knockout recovery: ejection now clears linear/angular momentum, knockdown/roll/sprint state and animation attributes, restores upright control and automatic network ownership, while preserving dodge cooldown and sprint budget. - November 9 occupied repair: overlap queries now use CanQuery rather than requiring character parts to collide, so queryable noncolliding occupants defer repair too. Reproduced the previous behavior with a modeled query response; all 18 actual service/timer mocked checks pass after the change. This follows [Roblox OverlapParams semantics](https://create.roblox.com/docs/reference/engine/datatypes/OverlapParams/RespectCanCollide); actual avatar geometry/collision-group behavior remains pending. Next: occupied-repair native check with collision-disabled character parts and ordinary residents.

- November 9 healing/balance consistency: introduced Shared.Healing as the common regeneration rule for the server and deterministic balance scenarios. Preserved 20% maximum-health healing after ten quiet seconds, one heal per observation without lag catch-up, and no resurrection. Damage/car-hit/join deadlines now use the shared quiet interval. Eight focused healing checks, all 70 existing balance scenarios and full verification/build pass (41 production scripts). Updated the migration harness to load the new real dependency. No balance tuning or native/real-voice result is claimed. Next: measure actual quiet-healing versus 4s/10s attack pacing before changing tuning.

- November 9 explicit voice arming: E/VOICE no longer arms an inactive microphone while a menu, dialogue, typing, protection, movement interruption, missing mic or lost focus makes gameplay unavailable. Next: test E while menus are open and explicit resume after closing, alongside real-mic checks.

- November 9 resident recovery UI state: residents now replicate ResidentActive on creation and health/recovery updates. Client target readouts, nearby labels/talk selection and open dialogue honor inactive residents even though their reusable Humanoid remains alive during recovery. This preserves the existing rig/recovery implementation and avoids presenting recovering NPCs as available once the flag arrives. Eighteen target and 25 dialogue mocked checks plus full verification/build pass; native attribute/position replication ordering remains pending. Next: KO a resident during dialogue/aim and verify recovery restores UI naturally without rig damage.

- November 9 talk input consistency: keyboard T and the Talk button now share requestTalk. Requests respect focus, typing, open menus/dialogue/test tools and microphone readiness; they revalidate the cached resident, active flag and current distance/cover through InteractionRules before sending. Valid requests cancel local capture immediately; server authority remains unchanged. Full verification/build passes; this is compile/integration evidence, not native input or raycast verification. Next: press T inside each panel and after a nearby NPC moves behind cover, then verify normal keyboard/touch conversation.

- November 9 beam teardown: sustained beam managers now have an idempotent terminal Destroy method; client script shutdown uses it. Existing Clear remains reusable, but updates after Destroy are rejected without creating visuals. Mock visual tests cover reuse after Clear, terminal teardown, repeated cleanup and late updates; full verification/build passes. No native instance/memory measurement is claimed. Next: verify cleanup while ending a local test or replacing the client script during active beams, then continue combat/effect lifecycle review.

- November 9 transient effect lifecycle: break/dodge/legacy scream effects now require a local character root before distance culling and allocation, matching the sustained beam path during respawn gaps. The remote effect listener is explicitly disconnected when the client script is destroyed. Full verification/build passes; no native memory/FPS improvement is claimed and the source/build checks do not establish runtime event ordering. Next: observe effect instance cleanup across respawn and local-test teardown, then continue combat/city readability review.

- November 9 attack-controller teardown: SustainedControls now supports terminal/idempotent Destroy, which disarms and cancels any pending/active local attack with one stop request. Client script destruction invokes it; late arming, availability and step callbacks cannot restart attacks. Four additional synthetic checks pass (24 control checks total), alongside full verification/build. Server input expiry remains the fallback if stop delivery is impossible. No native teardown/network delivery claim. Next: end a disposable test during a scream and verify server expiry/stop plus local cleanup, then continue core review.

- November 9 departing session ownership: removes gameplay session ownership before the final profile save yields, after closing dialogue and canceling both capture modes. Delayed gameplay callbacks cannot continue through sessions[p], and late save completion cannot delete a replacement session. Detached saves are counted separately in the existing bounded shutdown wait; protected save calls release the counter on exceptions. Legitimate developer-test baselines and save eligibility remain unchanged. Twelve actual-helper checks with mocked yielding saves and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 25 mocked-service suites); no real DataStore/shutdown evidence. Next: native leave/shutdown persistence validation using disposable test accounts/place before release.

- November 9 shutdown save cleanup: the shutdown callback now protects each save so exceptions release its pending counter and emit a warning. The 25-second deadline is set before launching saves; existing legitimate/test-baseline selection and waiting for detached departure saves are preserved. Eight mocked scheduler/save checks cover empty shutdown, temporary-profile exclusion, baseline/release arguments, exceptions, departure waiting and bounded hung work; full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 26 mocked-service suites). No actual DataStore durability or Roblox shutdown timing claim. Next: native shutdown/leave persistence gate before release; continue independent core work.

- November 9 autosave status recovery: unexpected Profiles.Save exceptions now enter the existing save-delayed/retry status instead of terminating the callback before status updates. Normal ownership/unavailable failures still disable saving; successful retries clear the delayed flag. Eleven mocked actual-helper checks cover exception notice suppression, retry recovery, permanent failures, legitimate test baselines and stale yielded completions; full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 27 mocked-service suites). No actual DataStore durability claim. Next: native persistence/failure-status validation before release; continue independent combat/city work.

- November 9 progression readability: Scream Collection now includes a content-sized summary of the next scream style and destructible tier, derived from the current definitions instead of duplicating level thresholds. Clarifies that level/live mic power set strength while styles change visuals; max level shows MAX LEVEL instead of a next-level XP fraction. Existing menu invalidation refreshes the summary on level changes. Full verification/build passes; native desktop/mobile layout and unlock-boundary presentation remain pending. Next: inspect levels 9/10, 25/26, 99/100 and small-screen scrolling in Studio; continue core/city readability improvements.

- November 9 quest input clarity: shared gardening/beach descriptions and gardener dialogue now reference the interaction prompt instead of requiring keyboard F. Locked quest guidance points to Talk, and the Talk button hides the T shortcut on touch devices. This changes guidance only; quest progress, rewards and interaction authority are unchanged. Full verification/build passes; actual mobile prompt activation/text fit remains pending. Next: complete garden/beach onboarding on desktop and touch, then continue combat/city interaction improvements.

- November 9 quest prompt availability: server quest callbacks now reject disabled prompts and prompts outside the active city before any progress/collection mutation. Existing distance, living-character, completion and duplicate-object checks remain. Nine actual-handler mocked checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 28 mocked-service suites). Native ProximityPrompt event ordering/activation remains pending. Next: disable/remove a quest prompt during interaction in a disposable fixture and verify no reward, then re-enable and complete normally.

- November 9 race attempt stability: repeated RaceStart interactions no longer reset an active attempt or extend its deadline. Start and deadline share one clock sample. Six added actual-handler mock checks cover repeated starts, minimum finish time, valid completion, duplicate finishes and completed-task restart; all 15 prompt checks and full verification/build pass. Native race-route timing/feel remains pending; rewards and race duration unchanged. Next: playtest repeated prompt activation and full boardwalk route on keyboard/touch before tuning timing.

- November 9 delivery regression coverage: added eight actual prompt-handler mocked checks for dropoff without pickup, route/deadline creation, duplicate pickup, wrong destination, expiry, exact-deadline completion, duplicate dropoff and completed-task pickup rejection. All 23 quest prompt checks pass; production code and timing/rewards unchanged. Simulated timeout/reaccept setup is labeled explicitly and is not a real route or scheduler test. Next: native assigned-route navigation and deadline playtest; continue independent game improvements.

- November 9 map arena consistency: the map arena fill, outline and displayed PvP share now derive from Config.ZoneMin/ZoneMax through the existing land-map projection, rather than separate hard-coded 40% rectangle values. Current footprint remains 40%; no world/combat boundary changed. Full verification/build passes, including dependency-aware harness builds. This prevents arena-only resizing from leaving stale map bounds; the schematic street/land footprint still describes the existing city. Next: compare map outline against native boundary markers and inspect future arena edits with the map open.

- November 9 PvP reward branch coverage: nine checks execute the actual sustained player-hit branch with mocked health/players/reward callbacks. Covers nonlethal damage/quiet-healing deadline, overkill-clamped hit feedback, KO recovery call, per-opponent 30-second reward cooldown and exact boundary, independent victims, victim-level XP input and Underdog eligibility. Focused suite passes and is registered in the unified runner. XP formula itself remains covered separately; this fixture stubs it to detect the supplied victim level. Production balance unchanged; no native PvP/geometry/network evidence. Next: native two-player KO/reentry and rewards playtest when available.

- November 9 sustained PvP KO confirmation: the attacker now receives an Arena KO notice naming the opponent, with either reward earned or repeat-opponent reward cooldown. Nonlethal hits keep their existing damage feedback; reward quantities/cooldowns are unchanged. Twelve actual PvP-branch mocked checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 29 mocked-service suites). Native toast timing/readability and multiplayer remain pending. Next: playtest KO/reentry and confirmation text without obscuring the next combat action.

- November 9 destruction confirmation: sustained screams now show one named successful-destruction notice per attack, explaining automatic repair. This is separate from the existing one-per-attack level-lock notice, so either event can follow the other without suppression. Rejected damage never announces a break; repeated observations cannot spam notices. Fifty synthetic result/helper checks and full verification/build pass. Native toast/readability remains pending; destruction balance/repair timing unchanged. Next: break unlocked props after hitting locked objects and inspect feedback during actual combat.

- November 9 inactive resident animation: local resident rigs now honor ResidentActive=false before pose calculations. Suspension clears cached limb/root/neck transforms, movement blend and elapsed time, and hides the local practice ball once while preserving reusable visuals. Active residents return to the normal update path on recovery. Three added mocked frame checks pass (10 resident lifecycle checks total), with full verification/build. No native animation appearance or measured performance claim. Next: observe repeated Nick/worker KO and recovery for clean pose/prop transitions.

- November 9 partial resident streaming: incomplete or out-of-workspace resident rigs now suspend poses and hide reusable practice props, using the same cleanup as recovery. Previously these frame branches skipped updates while leaving a detached local ball visible. Four added actual-frame mocked checks pass (14 resident lifecycle checks), plus full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 29 mocked-service suites). No native streaming/appearance claim. Next: approach/leave Nick across streaming boundaries and confirm his practice prop resumes with the complete rig.

- November 9 arena HUD focus: untimed city-task tracking now hides while the replicated player state is inside the arena. Combat/training objectives and active quest countdowns remain visible; leaving the arena restores ordinary tracking, and the Quests menu remains available. Quest progression/rewards are unchanged. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 29 mocked-service suites); no native layout/transition test is claimed. Next: enter/leave the arena with gardening, delivery countdown and Underdog tracked on desktop/mobile; inspect combat visibility and timer continuity.

- November 9 sprint interruption: client sprint requests now cancel on PlatformStand, Sit or an occupied SeatPart, matching existing death/knockdown interruption behavior. Reproduced a retained sprint request in the actual-helper mock before fixing it; recovery no longer silently retains that local request. Nine added checks cover cancellation, rejected retries and no automatic resume (43 sprint input checks total), and full verification/build passes. Server sprint authority/tuning unchanged; physical seat/ragdoll/input ordering remains pending. Next: sprint into a seat or ragdoll, recover, and verify a fresh keyboard/touch request is needed.

- November 9 server sprint eligibility: stepSprint now independently rejects an occupied SeatPart and anchored root, even if Sit is false or reported horizontal velocity remains nonzero. Reproduced the seated-state stamina drain in the actual-helper synthetic harness before fixing it. Seventeen server sprint checks and full verification/build pass; duration/recovery tuning unchanged. No real seat replication or physics result claimed. Next: native moving-seat and anchored-character checks alongside the client interruption sequence.

- November 9 duplicate quest acceptance: accepting an already active task now preserves its current attempt and gives a continuation notice instead of clearing progress, collection state or delivery/race deadlines. Reproduced progress loss in the actual acceptance branch with mocked callbacks. Eight regression checks cover duplicate garden/timed tasks, earned rewards, switching, abandoned restart, claimed and locked tasks; full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 30 mocked-service suites). Native menu/remote ordering and Nick task integration remain pending. Next: double-activate Accept during each task and verify intentional Abandon/reaccept still restarts.

- November 9 quest reward regression coverage: eight actual claim-branch mocked checks cover invalid/missing/incomplete tasks, normal completion, repeated/reentrant claim attempts, Nick achievement/reward, and claiming a previously earned reward without canceling a different active delivery. All pass; registered in the unified runner. Production behavior and reward tuning unchanged. Callback reentry is a synthetic ordering probe, not network/save evidence. Next: native claim/rejoin persistence and pending quest-UI checks; continue core development.

- November 9 conversation ownership regression coverage: seven actual server close-helper mocked checks cover current owner cleanup, stale/repeated close requests after ownership transfer, ambient social windows and departed sessions. All pass with full local verification (16 pure/synthetic, 32 mocked-service suites, 41 scripts compiled). Production behavior unchanged. This is sequential mocked ownership ordering, not native multiplayer timing. Next: two-player dialogue handoff and late closure playtest when available; continue independent core/city work.

- November 9 landmark loader teardown: local landmark loading now has a terminal destruction guard, disconnects collection listeners and restores fallback visibility for tracked models. Pending asset completions cannot swap visuals after teardown; late registration cannot launch loads. Six actual-script checks with deferred mocked preload callbacks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 33 mocked-service suites). Actual asset/streaming appearance remains pending; no measured performance claim. Next: remove/restart the loader during a pending asset load in a disposable place and inspect fallback continuity.

- November 9 landmark failure/re-entry coverage: added actual-loader mocked checks for failed fetch status, preload exceptions, old in-flight completion after tag removal/re-entry, and successful fresh loading. Added a pre-work ticket/destruction guard after a deferred-task mock showed obsolete queued work still invoked PreloadAsync. Eleven loader checks and full verification/build pass. The queued-task scheduling and coroutine preload are synthetic probes; actual Roblox scheduling/CDN/streaming remain unverified. Next: native delayed-load/re-entry visual check alongside pending teardown test.

- November 9 voice onset readability: automatic-mode mic meter now has a small threshold marker derived from the actual VoiceActivation.OnPower setting. Armed guidance names that percentage, and accumulating onset shows “Voice detected · keep speaking” before server-start acknowledgement. Existing quiet/cooldown/interruption messages retain priority. No attack thresholds/timing or detection behavior changed. Full verification/build passes; native text fit and real microphone usability remain pending. Next: inspect threshold alignment and onset/quiet feedback on desktop/mobile with comfortable calibrated voice.

- November 9 ambience startup cancellation: added destruction checks after waiting for ScreamRemotes and Action, preventing late startup from sending Sync after teardown. Reproduced the late request with the actual remote-hookup tail and coroutine waits; nine checks cover cancellation at each wait, ordinary startup and volume validation. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 34 mocked-service suites). Teardown is simulated in the harness; no audio/device or native scheduler result. Next: native startup/teardown with delayed remotes and city audio settings.

- November 9 occupied-repair feedback: destruction service now replicates RepairBlocked when an occupied automatic repair retries. Initialization, accepted damage and successful restoration clear it. Damaged-object target UI explains “Repair waiting · clear the area” instead of showing only a repeatedly renewed countdown. Four added mocked service checks pass (45 total), plus full verification/build. Timing, occupancy and damage balance unchanged; native replication/text fit remains pending. Next: stand in a damaged prop repair volume, observe the message, then leave and verify restoration clears it.

- November 9 performance workload context: disposable PerformanceReview before/after snapshots now include active sustained attacks, working/recovering residents and damaged/destroyed/repair-blocked objects, excluding tagged models outside Workspace. Five actual snapshot-helper mocked checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 35 mocked-service suites). No production script changed and no performance sample was measured. These are endpoint counts, not peak or average workload during the interval. Next: run labeled idle/combat/destruction native samples and record workload snapshots alongside heartbeat timing before optimization.

- November 9 KO cooldown retention: shared claimKoReward now handles legacy/sustained reward eligibility and removes expired victim entries at most once per 30 seconds when a KO reward check occurs. Unexpired entries survive opponent departure/rejoin; reward amount and exact deadline eligibility remain unchanged. Nine synthetic helper checks include 1,000 expired historical entries, plus all 12 mocked PvP branch checks and full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 36 mocked-service suites). This establishes cleanup behavior, not measured memory/FPS improvement. Next: native repeat-opponent/rejoin and long-session checks; continue combat review.

- November 9 pooled scream effect cleanup: ScreamEffects.Clear cancels active tweens, destroys live/cached parts and resets budgets; Client teardown invokes it. Delayed expiry callbacks verify their live tween identity before pooling, so old work cannot revive destroyed parts or alter a later lifecycle. Clear stays reusable because the required module may be cached across client script replacement. Nine actual particle/pool checks with mocked tweens and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 37 mocked-service suites). No native instance/memory/rendering measurement. Next: end/restart a disposable client during legacy cosmetic effects and inspect cleanup/reuse.

- November 9 hit feedback across respawn: CharacterRemoving now clears visible confirmed damage and discards feedback through the last locally started attack sequence. HitFeedback retains a monotonic discard floor, rejecting late confirmations from pre-removal attacks while accepting fresh later sequences. Six added pure checks pass (20 total), plus full verification/build. Server damage/rewards/cooldowns unchanged; no native respawn/network ordering claim. Next: respawn while damaging a target under latency and verify old confirmations do not reappear, then attack after recovery.

- November 9 aimed-target cover preview: player/NPC readouts now show BEHIND COVER and muted availability when a local collidable ray from the character scream origin to the target root is blocked, even if the camera can see over cover. Reuses server-style head/root origin and exclusion semantics; existing safe/protection/dodge/range status priority remains. Checks occur only during the existing throttled target readout and inside maximum range. Five added presentation checks pass (23 total), plus full verification/build. Ray geometry/streaming/native performance remain untested; this preview never grants damage or guarantees server acceptance. Next: third-person over-wall versus first-person targeting with actual cover and streamed geometry.

- November 9 cover preview contract coverage: added actual client targetCovered and server attackOrigin/audible comparisons with mocked vectors/raycast. Six head states (valid, near-limit, exact limit, displaced, wrong class, missing), each blocked/clear, verify matching origin/direction, character/target exclusions and collidable-query settings (60 assertions). All pass; production code unchanged. This checks setup/interpretation only, not engine geometry or server beam-width acceptance. Next: native over-wall, corner and streamed-obstacle aiming tests.

- November 9 seated player overlay: RunAnimation now yields when SeatPart is occupied independently of Sit. Existing interruption cleanup clears charge/sprint blends, landing state and roll/sprint visual attributes, matching the recently hardened movement checks. Three added mocked frame assertions pass (26 player lifecycle checks; 14 resident checks), with full verification/build. Native seat replication/Animator appearance remains pending; attack eligibility and gameplay tuning unchanged. Next: inspect R6/R15 seated transitions with sprint/scream attributes set and separate Sit/SeatPart arrival.

- November 9 sustained control recovery coverage: six additional synthetic checks exercise actual controls/gate code for a long frame interval without catch-up pulse bursts, camera loss during acknowledged attack, no held-voice retrigger on camera return, fresh quiet/onset recovery, invalid power cancellation and server-active state without local attack ownership. All 30 controller checks and full local verification pass. Production timing/behavior unchanged; no device, real frame stall or network claim. Next: native camera replacement/frame-stall recovery and real-mic interruption tests.

- November 9 contributor/release documentation reconciliation: README and contributor controls now distinguish current automatic voice from City Preview v19 hold/release, correct stale current-level claims, link the preview separately from the original experience, and recommend guarded temporary-profile fixtures for local review. Replaced conflicting historical release recovery instructions with a current handoff that marks v19 authorization completed and requires fresh upload approval. Local Markdown links resolve and diff checks pass; documentation-only, no runtime test or publication claimed. Next: use the corrected fixture/controls instructions for owner playtesting, then continue prioritized implementation.

- November 9 main HUD teardown: Client destruction now destroys its main ScreenGui and invalidates pending menu scroll-restoration revisions. Five actual teardown/callback mocked checks cover normal restoration, bounds, newer-render ordering and cleanup. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 39 mocked-service suites). No native GUI/event-lifetime result; this is scoped main-HUD cleanup, not a claim that every client subscription lifecycle has been audited. Next: remove/restart Client with an open scrolled menu and confirm no stale HUD or late scroll errors.

- November 9 microphone access timeout coverage: five additional mocked lifecycle checks verify stale timeout rejection after retry, current timeout guidance without attack readiness, eventual successful access recovery, preservation of completed success and no mutation after controller destruction. All 43 microphone checks and full local verification pass (16 pure/synthetic, 39 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). Production behavior unchanged; no real device/network access result. Next: test slow voice eligibility and retry/reconnect on an eligible account, then continue combat correctness review.

- November 9 client external-signal lifecycle: main HUD frame/state, keyboard/focus/menu, character-removal, protection and jump listeners now share tracked cleanup. Destruction disconnects them, clears retained connection references and suppresses queued callbacks; late registration is rejected. Six mocked signal-dispatch checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 40 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). Existing effect/controller teardown stays separate. No native scheduling, memory or hot-reload result; startup yields and every other module are outside this scoped change. Next: replace/remove Client during an active attack/open menu in a disposable place and inspect old HUD updates/input packets, then continue combat review.

- November 9 microphone analyzer recovery: FindInput now reuses its cached input only while the analyzer remains parented. Removed/destroyed analyzer recovery rebuilds the meter through the existing reset path, cancels capture and requires fresh calibration. A new mocked removal test failed before the fix and passes afterward; all 50 microphone lifecycle checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 40 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No device/audio-engine recovery claim. Next: remove the local meter during capture in a disposable Studio session and verify calibration/re-arm behavior with actual eligible voice input.

- November 9 microphone wire recovery: cached meter reuse now validates its owned Wire parent, source input and target analyzer. Broken/misrouted links rebuild the meter through cancellation and fresh-calibration flow. Explicit wire destruction also cleans up detached wires during rebuild/teardown. Mocked detached-wire reproduction failed before the fix; removal, source mismatch and target mismatch now pass with recalibration (74 microphone lifecycle checks). Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 40 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). Endpoint validation is not evidence of actual audio flow or voice transport. Next: native eligible-account wire removal/reconnection tests, then continue combat/destruction review.

- November 9 destruction registration restart safety: Service.Start advances a generation captured by tag callbacks and deferred registration. Old callbacks cannot register objects into or remove objects from a newer registry, including same-world restarts. Mocked stale deferred registration failed before the fix; six lifecycle assertions now pass (51 destruction service checks), with full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 40 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). Damage/repair balance unchanged. No native tag-event ordering or hot-reload evidence. Next: disposable Studio service restart/tag churn check, then continued combat/destruction correctness review.

- November 9 voice timing readability: voice action button now includes a compact timing bar, green remaining duration during a server-active attack and muted recovery progress during cooldown. Uses replicated deadlines and shared duration/cooldown constants, clamps fill, and hides when dead/protected or idle. Existing live-power meter and text countdown remain separate; no combat tuning/input changes. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 40 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled); UI harness compiled but was not visually executed. Next: inspect desktop/mobile bar contrast, deadline replication and button tap behavior in a disposable native playtest.

- November 9 incomplete resident label cleanup: refreshResidentLabels now hides available head billboards before rejecting a missing root. An NPC partially removed/streamed no longer retains its previous name/speech display, and selection recovers once complete. Mocked missing-root reproduction failed before the fix; six actual-function label checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 41 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No native streaming/rendering evidence. Next: observe partial resident streaming and label recovery on a disposable client; continue NPC/UI correctness review.

- November 9 interaction recovery gate: shared CanReachNpc rejects PlatformStand on either participant before performing cover checks. Client talk selection/request and server conversation/vendor validation use the same rule, preventing new interactions during ragdoll recovery and allowing them again when recovery ends. Mocked knocked-player reproduction failed before the fix; six new checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 42 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No native car/physics/state replication evidence; existing dialogue lifetime and dodge eligibility unchanged. Next: native car knockdown near a quest/vendor resident, verify talk unavailable until recovery and no unintended purchase.

- November 9 open-dialogue knockdown cleanup: refreshConversation now closes when the player or resident enters PlatformStand, using existing EndConversation cleanup rather than leaving choices visible after the shared interaction rule rejects them. Mocked player-knockdown reproduction failed before the fix; four added assertions pass (29 dialogue lifecycle checks), with full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 42 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No real physics/UI/network evidence; quests, rewards and attack timing unchanged. Next: native knockdown with an open vendor/quest conversation, verify closure and fresh interaction after recovery.

- November 9 busker ambience lifecycle: guitar activity now requires an active resident inside Workspace, in addition to existing role/work/health/distance checks. Inactive or removed performers fade out; returning active performers resume normally. Queued heartbeat/settings callbacks return after teardown. Mocked queued-state reproduction failed before the fix; 15 combined startup/activity checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 42 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No actual playback, fade quality or audio performance claim. Next: native busker work/recovery/streaming transitions and ambience teardown listening check.

- November 9 coastal map navigation: CityMap now draws the waterfront promenade and timber pier using the current City geometry dimensions/positions, adds pier landmark 7 and explains the promenade color. World geometry, routes and PvP bounds unchanged. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 42 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled); UI preview compiled, not rendered/visually verified. Next: check mobile legend wrapping/pin readability and compare map position while walking the promenade and pier in native play.

- November 9 map marker lifecycle: detached player roots now hide the marker, and newly created markers remain hidden until their first valid update. A mocked detached-root reproduction failed before the fix; nine marker checks cover projection, north/east/west heading, bounds, disappearance/recovery and removed maps. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 43 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No native respawn/rendering claim. Next: open Map across respawn and verify no stale corner/old-character marker, then inspect coastal landmark layout.

- November 9 queued combat effect cleanup: the shared Effect remote listener now uses connectClient, which disconnects on teardown and rejects callbacks queued before destruction. This extends the existing terminal beam-manager protection to break/dodge particles, legacy cosmetic effects and hit feedback. Removed redundant standalone disconnect. Existing six mocked connection tests, beam/pool lifecycle suites and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 43 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No native queued-event/particle evidence or performance claim. Next: disposable client replacement during effects and confirm no particles appear from the old listener.

- November 9 owner playtest triage: added a prioritized six-step smoke test and result-recording format at the top of AUTOMATIC_VOICE_PLAYTEST, separating local temporary-profile review from real voice/listener, legitimate persistence and measured performance checks. Documentation-only; no runtime checks newly claimed, no pending checks marked passed and no publication. Next: owner runs the smoke test when available; continue independent implementation with failures prioritized when reported.

- November 9 UI preview isolation integrity: builder now requires exactly one match for microphone, remote and GUI substitutions, stopping on source drift rather than silently retaining real dependencies. Mock remote subscriptions now return disconnect handles compatible with client teardown. Actual replacement-helper checks pass for exact, absent and duplicate matches; full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 43 mocked-service suites). Production source unchanged; generated preview compiled but not executed in Studio. Next: native layout-only preview and recorded clipping results when Studio is available.

- November 9 responsive progression label: shared progressLabel selection now applies on server state updates and viewport refresh, restoring XP/MAX LEVEL when moving from compact save-status display to wide layout without waiting for another state packet. Compact temporary/test status retains priority over delayed saves. Only changed text is reassigned during frame updates. Eight mocked selector checks and full verification/build pass (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). No native resizing/rendering or save evidence. Next: resize a temporary/delayed-save HUD across 820px and verify XP/status placement, including level 100.

- November 9 quest reward visibility: Quests button now shows a gold unclaimed-completion count from each server state snapshot, returning to its normal label after claims. Counts only defined quests with earned, unclaimed rewards; does not grant rewards or alter quest state. Full verification/build passes (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites, 41 production scripts compiled). Native count updates, visual fit and mobile readability remain pending. - November 9 Nick quest control consistency: active Nick quest now displays TRACKING and avoids sending duplicate AcceptQuest requests, matching the server's existing active-attempt preservation. Full verification/build passes, including existing acceptance/claim suites (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites). No server progression/reward changes or native UI verification. Next: confirm active Nick stages/timers survive menu taps and explicit abandonment still restarts cleanly in a disposable playtest.

- November 9 active quest claim shortcut: completed active tasks now show COMPLETE and CLAIM in the top quest summary; unfinished tasks retain CURRENT and ABANDON. Uses the existing authoritative ClaimQuest path and exact captured quest ID; no reward/progression changes. Full verification/build passes, including existing repeated-claim/reentrancy checks (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites). Native button/layout/state replication remains pending. Next: complete an active task, claim from the summary and verify a single reward and cleared active task, then verify unfinished abandonment.

Calibrate before attacking.” Previously readiness was correctly cleared but the setup panel could still display its prior ready status. Mocked analyzer-recovery assertion failed before the fix; four added recovery-message assertions pass (78 microphone lifecycle checks), with full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites). No actual audio/device verification. Next: native input/analyzer/wire recovery should show calibration guidance until fresh calibration completes.

- November 9 interrupted-calibration unmute guidance: Microphone tracks an observed mute transition and replaces the stale muted status with a calibration prompt on unmute when no valid calibration/active calibration exists. Existing valid calibration still resumes normally; no automatic attack re-arm changes. Mocked quiet/voice-phase interruption assertions failed before the fix and pass afterward (80 microphone lifecycle checks), with full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites). No device verification. Next: mute during each calibration phase, unmute and verify the prompt directs a fresh attempt without claiming the microphone is still muted.

- November 9 player animation component gaps: additive player poses now enter existing interruption cleanup when the cached root or humanoid is detached, clearing charge/sprint/landing state and visual indicators. Six added mocked interruption assertions pass (32 player animation lifecycle checks), with full verification/build (16 pure/synthetic, 44 mocked-service suites). Pre-fix mock entered unsupported pose code instead of interruption cleanup; this is not a demonstrated native exception. No native Animator/streaming appearance result; replacement-object rebinding is outside this scoped change. - November 9 pending-access calibration guard: Calibrate returns while voice eligibility is still pending, preserving checking/timeout guidance and avoiding unnecessary input-graph allocation before a result. ## Academy art and controls playtest, November 18

Reworked the compact Academy against the approved MAP-01 campus reference. The palace has warm plaster, teal roofs, framed glazing and a distinct tower. The library backdrop leaves a central opening. Cypress groups and planting beds frame the fountain. Inset shore terraces, a lower ridge and basalt caldera faces replace the haphazard coastal blocks. The final compact export has 3,085 parts, 106 routes and all eleven bridges.

The supplied level-one clip shows an active beam while facing the level-ten encounter. Guidance now identifies the practice resonator and center reticle. Consumed UI input is excluded. Flight movement steps every server Heartbeat, separate from combat; keyboard and touch flight inputs are selected independently on hybrid devices. A disposable native Studio playtest using the revised scripts reported 2,622 floor and 2,613 headroom probes with zero failures. Automated Windows left-mouse hold and release produced active beam then cooldown; with flight enabled through the fixture, one second of Space moved the avatar 32 studs. This does not measure network jitter, physical touch, or human comfort. Geometry verification passed 14,538 route samples, 135 Academy probes and 2,205 boss probes. Next review player-height views, the new coast and steering feel with the owner.

## Bounded voice onset trial

- Lowered the calibrated start gate from 0.35 to 0.33, leaving the 0.18 release level, 0.25 s sustained onset, 0.20 s quiet/release interval, off toggle and five-second cooldown unchanged. The meter already reads the shared start gate. The independently applied server input floor remains 0.08 for start and sustain; no server settings or protocol changes.
- Synthetic controller traces pass the new boundary, sustained input below onset, sub-threshold/short interrupted peaks, quiet after cooldown and re-arm, and absolute off. This amplitude gate does not classify speech: sustained speech in the newly admitted band may trigger while on. Next: compare ordinary speech and deliberate mic activation on the same device/calibration in Studio, without storing voice recordings. No physical mic or native execution claimed.
